================================================================================
RPG AGENT JCL VAULT — POLYNOMIAL TURING MACHINE SPECIFICATION
Binary-Level Reference Document (BLRD) — Revision 1.0
================================================================================

DOCUMENT ID: BLRD-PTM-2026-001
VAULT BOUNDARY: /mnt/data/jcl_vault/
CLASSICAL LAYER: Deterministic Polynomial Turing Machine
QUANTUM LAYER: Quipper Circuit Representation
SOURCE FAMILY: Fortran 77 through Fortran 95/97
MINIMUM LINE COUNT: 3300

NOTICE: This document specifies every artifact at the bit and byte level.
No implementation language is assumed. All formats are language-neutral.

================================================================================

SECTION 0000: BINARY FOUNDATION
================================================================================

0001 The entire pipeline operates on sequences of bits.
0002 A bit is the atomic unit of information with value 0 or value 1.
0003 A byte is an ordered sequence of exactly eight bits: b7 b6 b5 b4 b3 b2 b1 b0.
0004 b7 is the most significant bit (MSB) of the byte.
0005 b0 is the least significant bit (LSB) of the byte.
0006 All multi-byte words use big-endian byte ordering unless explicitly overridden.
0007 Big-endian ordering places the most significant byte at the lowest address.
0008 Little-endian ordering places the least significant byte at the lowest address.
0009 The canonical word size for PTM-IR is 32 bits (four bytes).
0010 The extended word size for PTM-IR is 64 bits (eight bytes).
0011 Address fields within PTM-IR use the extended word size.
0012 Counter fields within PTM-IR use the canonical word size.
0013 Flag fields within PTM-IR use single bytes.
0014 A nibble is four bits (half-byte) and is used for packed BCD representations.
0015 The binary alphabet for tape cells is {0, 1, B} where B is the blank symbol.
0016 The blank symbol B is encoded as the byte 0x00 in binary tape dumps.
0017 The symbol 0 is encoded as the byte 0x30 in binary tape dumps.
0018 The symbol 1 is encoded as the byte 0x31 in binary tape dumps.
0019 Bit strings are written left-to-right with index 0 at the leftmost position.
0020 Bit string concatenation is denoted by the vertical bar |.
0021 For bit string x and integer n, x^n denotes n concatenated copies of x.
0022 The empty bit string is denoted epsilon and has length zero.
0023 The length of bit string x is denoted |x| in bits.
0024 The byte-length of bit string x is ceil(|x| / 8).
0025 Padding bits, when required, are appended as zeros to the right.
0026 All checksums are computed over the raw byte sequence before padding.
0027 The canonical hash function for audit records is SHA-256.
0028 SHA-256 produces a 256-bit (32-byte) digest.
0029 SHA-256 digests are serialized as 64 hexadecimal characters.
0030 Hexadecimal notation uses uppercase A-F for values 10-15.
0031 Binary notation uses prefix 0b. Hexadecimal uses prefix 0x.
0032 Octal notation is not used in this specification.
0033 Signed integers use two's complement representation.
0034 A 32-bit two's complement integer ranges from -2,147,483,648 to +2,147,483,647.
0035 A 64-bit two's complement integer ranges from -9,223,372,036,854,775,808 to +9,223,372,036,854,775,807.
0036 Floating-point values use IEEE 754 binary64 (double precision) format.
0037 IEEE 754 binary64 occupies 64 bits: 1 sign bit, 11 exponent bits, 52 mantissa bits.
0038 The exponent bias for binary64 is 1023.
0039 Special values (NaN, Infinity, -Infinity) are preserved through PTM-IR.
0040 Denormalized numbers are preserved and not flushed to zero.
0041 Boolean values are encoded as single bytes: 0x00 for FALSE, 0x01 for TRUE.
0042 Any byte value other than 0x00 or 0x01 in a Boolean field is a validation error.
0043 Character data uses ASCII encoding for the canonical pipeline.
0044 ASCII is a 7-bit encoding mapped into the low seven bits of each byte.
0045 The high bit (bit 7) of an ASCII byte must be zero.
0046 EBCDIC source inputs must be transcoded to ASCII before lexical analysis.
0047 EBCDIC transcoding uses the standard IBM Code Page 037 mapping table.
0048 The transcoding table is part of the vault configuration and is read-only.
0049 The transcoding table hash is recorded in the audit manifest.
0050 Unicode source inputs are not supported in this revision of the specification.
0051 All offsets and addresses are zero-based unless explicitly stated otherwise.
0052 A sector is 512 bytes. A block is 4096 bytes.
0053 File sizes are measured in bytes, not blocks or sectors.
0054 Memory alignment for 32-bit words is to addresses divisible by four.
0055 Memory alignment for 64-bit words is to addresses divisible by eight.
0056 Unaligned access is prohibited and triggers a validation failure.
0057 Bit fields within a byte are numbered from 7 (MSB) down to 0 (LSB).
0058 Bit masks are specified as hexadecimal values applied with bitwise AND.
0059 Bit extraction uses shift-right followed by bitwise AND with the mask.
0060 All arithmetic on bit indices uses unsigned 32-bit integers.
0061 Overflow in bit-index arithmetic is defined as modulo 2^32.
0062 Underflow in bit-index arithmetic is defined as modulo 2^32.
0063 The bit-order for serializing structures is MSB-first within each field.
0064 Fields within a structure are serialized in declaration order.
0065 No implicit padding is inserted between fields unless specified.
0066 Explicit padding fields are named pad_N where N is the byte count.
0067 All magic numbers in file headers are four-byte big-endian values.
0068 The magic number for PTM-IR files is 0x50544D49 ('PTMI').
0069 The magic number for Turing Machine transition tables is 0x544D5454 ('TMTT').
0070 The magic number for Quipper artifacts is 0x51554950 ('QUIP').
0071 The magic number for JCL manifests is 0x4A434C4D ('JCLM').
0072 The magic number for audit records is 0x41554454 ('AUDT').
0073 The magic number for test vectors is 0x54455354 ('TEST').
0074 File headers include a version field immediately after the magic number.
0075 The version field is a 16-bit unsigned integer in big-endian format.
0076 The current version for all artifact types is 0x0001.
0077 File headers include a flags field after the version field.
0078 The flags field is a 16-bit unsigned integer in big-endian format.
0079 Bit 15 of the flags field indicates whether the file is encrypted (1) or plaintext (0).
0080 Bit 14 of the flags field indicates whether the file is compressed (1) or raw (0).
0081 Bits 13 through 0 of the flags field are reserved and must be zero.
0082 A reserved bit set to one triggers a validation failure.
0083 The header length is fixed at 16 bytes for all artifact types.
0084 Bytes 0-3 of the header contain the magic number.
0085 Bytes 4-5 of the header contain the version.
0086 Bytes 6-7 of the header contain the flags.
0087 Bytes 8-11 of the header contain the payload length as a 32-bit big-endian integer.
0088 Bytes 12-15 of the header contain the header checksum (CRC-32).
0089 The CRC-32 is computed over bytes 0-11 of the header.
0090 The CRC-32 polynomial is 0x04C11DB7 per ISO 3309.
0091 The CRC-32 initial value is 0xFFFFFFFF.
0092 The CRC-32 final XOR value is 0xFFFFFFFF.
0093 The payload follows immediately after the 16-byte header.
0094 The payload length does not include the header bytes.
0095 The total file size is exactly header length plus payload length.
0096 Trailing bytes after the payload are prohibited.
0097 Any trailing byte detected triggers a validation failure.
0098 Empty payloads (length zero) are permitted for placeholder artifacts.
0099 A placeholder artifact must have the placeholder flag set in the header flags.
0100 The placeholder flag is bit 13 of the flags field, overriding the reserved-zero rule.

SECTION 0001: LEXICAL ENCODING
================================================================================

0101 The lexical analyzer reads the source file as a sequence of bytes.
0102 Each byte is classified into exactly one lexical category.
0103 The category of a byte is determined by its ASCII value.
0104 Byte values 0x00 through 0x1F are control characters.
0105 Control character 0x0A (LF) is the canonical line terminator.
0106 Control character 0x0D (CR) is an alternate line terminator.
0107 The sequence CR followed by LF (0x0D 0x0A) counts as a single line terminator.
0108 Control character 0x09 (HT) is the canonical horizontal tab.
0109 A tab advances the column counter to the next multiple of eight.
0110 Column counting is zero-based.
0111 Byte value 0x20 (SP) is the space character.
0112 Spaces separate tokens but are not themselves tokens.
0113 Byte values 0x21 through 0x2F are punctuation and operators.
0114 Byte values 0x3A through 0x40 are punctuation and operators.
0115 Byte values 0x5B through 0x60 are punctuation and operators.
0116 Byte values 0x7B through 0x7E are punctuation and operators.
0117 Byte values 0x30 through 0x39 are decimal digits.
0118 Byte values 0x41 through 0x5A are uppercase letters.
0119 Byte values 0x61 through 0x7A are lowercase letters.
0120 Fortran is case-insensitive; lowercase letters are mapped to uppercase before tokenization.
0121 The mapping is performed by clearing bit 5 of the ASCII byte (AND with 0xDF).
0122 Byte value 0x27 (apostrophe) initiates a character literal.
0123 Byte value 0x22 (quotation mark) initiates a Hollerith string in fixed-form Fortran.
0124 A comment begins with the letter C in column 1 of fixed-form source.
0125 In column 1, C is recognized before case mapping (0x43 or 0x63).
0126 A comment begins with an exclamation mark (!) in free-form source.
0127 Comment text extends to the end of the line and is discarded.
0128 Comment bytes do not participate in token emission.
0129 Line continuation in fixed form is indicated by a non-zero, non-blank character in column 6.
0130 Column 6 is byte index 5 of the line (zero-based).
0131 Lines shorter than six characters cannot contain a continuation marker in column 6.
0132 Line continuation in free form is indicated by an ampersand (&) at the end of a line.
0133 The ampersand must be the last non-whitespace character before the line terminator.
0134 A lexical token is represented as a 16-byte record.
0135 Bytes 0-3 of the token record contain the token type as a 32-bit big-endian integer.
0136 Token type values are assigned from the enumeration starting at 0x00000001.
0137 Token type 0x00000000 is reserved and indicates an uninitialized token.
0138 Token type 0x00000001 is TOK_EOF (end of file).
0139 Token type 0x00000002 is TOK_EOL (end of line).
0140 Token type 0x00000003 is TOK_IDENTIFIER.
0141 Token type 0x00000004 is TOK_INTEGER_LITERAL.
0142 Token type 0x00000005 is TOK_REAL_LITERAL.
0143 Token type 0x00000006 is TOK_CHARACTER_LITERAL.
0144 Token type 0x00000007 is TOK_LOGICAL_LITERAL.
0145 Token type 0x00000008 is TOK_OPERATOR.
0146 Token type 0x00000009 is TOK_KEYWORD.
0147 Token type 0x0000000A is TOK_DELIMITER.
0148 Token type 0x0000000B is TOK_LABEL.
0149 Token type 0x0000000C is TOK_UNKNOWN.
0150 Bytes 4-7 of the token record contain the line number (32-bit big-endian).
0151 Bytes 8-11 of the token record contain the column number (32-bit big-endian).
0152 Bytes 12-15 of the token record contain the token length in bytes (32-bit big-endian).
0153 The token text follows the 16-byte record in the token stream.
0154 Token text is not null-terminated.
0155 Token text length is derived from the length field, not from delimiter scanning.
0156 The token stream is a concatenation of token records and token texts.
0157 The token stream file uses the magic number 0x544F4B4E ('TOKN').
0158 Keyword recognition uses a perfect hash table over the uppercase keyword set.
0159 The perfect hash table is precomputed and stored in the vault as a read-only asset.
0160 The hash table asset hash is recorded in the audit manifest.
0161 Keywords include PROGRAM, SUBROUTINE, FUNCTION, INTEGER, REAL, LOGICAL, CHARACTER.
0162 Keywords include DIMENSION, COMMON, DATA, PARAMETER, IMPLICIT, EXTERNAL, INTRINSIC.
0163 Keywords include IF, THEN, ELSE, ELSEIF, ENDIF, DO, ENDDO, WHILE.
0164 Keywords include CONTINUE, GOTO, RETURN, STOP, PAUSE, CALL, READ, WRITE.
0165 Keywords include PRINT, OPEN, CLOSE, INQUIRE, BACKSPACE, REWIND, ENDFILE.
0166 Keywords include FORMAT, SAVE, EQUIVALENCE, ENTRY, BLOCK DATA.
0167 Keywords include ASSIGN, TO, GO, END, MODULE, USE, ONLY, INTERFACE.
0168 Keywords include OPERATOR, ASSIGNMENT, CONTAINS, RECURSIVE, PURE, ELEMENTAL.
0169 An identifier is a sequence of letters, digits, and underscores starting with a letter.
0170 The maximum identifier length is 31 characters.
0171 Identifiers longer than 31 characters are truncated with a validation warning.
0172 An integer literal is a sequence of decimal digits optionally preceded by a sign.
0173 The sign is tokenized as a separate operator, not part of the literal.
0174 A real literal contains a decimal point or an exponent part or both.
0175 The exponent part is the letter E followed by an optional sign and decimal digits.
0176 Real literals are tokenized as text and parsed to IEEE 754 binary64 during semantic analysis.
0177 A character literal is enclosed in apostrophes.
0178 An apostrophe within a character literal is represented by two consecutive apostrophes.
0179 The token text for a character literal includes the enclosing apostrophes.
0180 A logical literal is either .TRUE. or .FALSE. (case-insensitive).
0181 The token text for a logical literal includes the enclosing periods.
0182 Operators include +, -, *, /, **, //, .EQ., .NE., .LT., .LE., .GT., .GE.
0183 Operators include .AND., .OR., .NOT., .EQV., .NEQV.
0184 Operators include =, =>, (, ), ,, :, ::, %.
0185 The token type for an operator is TOK_OPERATOR with subtype in bytes 12-15.
0186 Operator subtype 0x00000001 is OP_ADD.
0187 Operator subtype 0x00000002 is OP_SUB.
0188 Operator subtype 0x00000003 is OP_MUL.
0189 Operator subtype 0x00000004 is OP_DIV.
0190 Operator subtype 0x00000005 is OP_POW.
0191 Operator subtype 0x00000006 is OP_CONCAT.
0192 Operator subtype 0x00000007 is OP_EQ.
0193 Operator subtype 0x00000008 is OP_NE.
0194 Operator subtype 0x00000009 is OP_LT.
0195 Operator subtype 0x0000000A is OP_LE.
0196 Operator subtype 0x0000000B is OP_GT.
0197 Operator subtype 0x0000000C is OP_GE.
0198 Operator subtype 0x0000000D is OP_AND.
0199 Operator subtype 0x0000000E is OP_OR.
0200 Operator subtype 0x0000000F is OP_NOT.
0201 Operator subtype 0x00000010 is OP_EQV.
0202 Operator subtype 0x00000011 is OP_NEQV.
0203 Operator subtype 0x00000012 is OP_ASSIGN.
0204 Operator subtype 0x00000013 is OP_ARROW.
0205 A label is a sequence of one to five decimal digits.
0206 Labels are tokenized as TOK_LABEL.
0207 A label value is stored as a 32-bit unsigned integer in the token subtype field.
0208 Label values greater than 99999 trigger a validation failure.
0209 Fixed-form columns 1-5 are scanned for labels before general tokenization.
0210 If columns 1-5 contain only spaces, no label is emitted for that line.
0211 If columns 1-5 contain a non-digit non-space character, it is a validation error.
0212 Column 6 in fixed form is reserved for continuation and is not part of any token.
0213 Columns 7-72 in fixed form contain the statement body.
0214 Columns 73-80 in fixed form are ignored (sequence numbers).
0215 Sequence number columns are discarded and do not participate in tokenization.
0216 Free-form lines have no column restrictions.
0217 Free-form statement length is limited to 132 characters per line.
0218 Lines exceeding 132 characters trigger a validation failure in free form.
0219 The lexical analyzer emits a token stream file as its sole output.
0220 The token stream file header uses magic 0x544F4B4E and version 0x0001.
0221 The token stream payload is the concatenation of all token records and texts.
0222 The token stream file is validated before parsing begins.
0223 Validation checks magic, version, header checksum, and payload length consistency.
0224 Validation checks that every token record points to text within the payload bounds.
0225 Validation checks that token types are within the defined enumeration range.
0226 Validation checks that line and column numbers are monotonic within reasonable bounds.
0227 Any lexical validation failure stops the pipeline with a fail-closed status.
0228 The lexical analyzer does not modify the source file.
0229 The lexical analyzer does not emit diagnostics to network resources.
0230 The lexical analyzer writes diagnostics to a vault-local log file.
0231 The diagnostic log file uses magic 0x44494147 ('DIAG').
0232 Each diagnostic record is 32 bytes: 4 bytes severity, 4 bytes line, 4 bytes column, 20 bytes message code.
0233 Severity 0x00000001 is INFO.
0234 Severity 0x00000002 is WARNING.
0235 Severity 0x00000003 is ERROR.
0236 Severity 0x00000004 is FATAL.
0237 A FATAL diagnostic aborts the pipeline immediately.
0238 An ERROR diagnostic aborts the pipeline after the current phase completes.
0239 A WARNING diagnostic does not abort but is recorded in the audit trail.
0240 An INFO diagnostic is recorded for traceability only.
0241 The lexical phase ends when the token stream file is written and validated.
0242 The token stream file hash is computed and recorded in the audit manifest.
0243 The source file hash is computed and recorded in the audit manifest.
0244 The source file hash is SHA-256 over the raw bytes of the source file.
0245 The token stream hash is SHA-256 over the complete token stream file.
0246 Both hashes are recorded before parsing begins.
0247 The lexical analyzer does not execute arbitrary code.
0248 The lexical analyzer does not access files outside the vault boundary.
0249 The lexical analyzer does not infer or request credentials.
0250 The lexical analyzer treats all source input as untrusted.

SECTION 0002: TYPE SYSTEM BINARY REPRESENTATION
================================================================================

0251 Every variable, constant, and expression has a type encoded in PTM-IR.
0252 The type descriptor is a fixed 16-byte structure.
0253 Byte 0 of the type descriptor contains the type class.
0254 Type class 0x01 is TC_INTEGER.
0255 Type class 0x02 is TC_REAL.
0256 Type class 0x03 is TC_LOGICAL.
0257 Type class 0x04 is TC_CHARACTER.
0258 Type class 0x05 is TC_ARRAY.
0259 Type class 0x06 is TC_DERIVED.
0260 Type class 0x07 is TC_POINTER.
0261 Type class 0x00 is TC_UNKNOWN (unresolved).
0262 Byte 1 of the type descriptor contains the kind parameter.
0263 Kind 0x01 is the default kind for the type class.
0264 Kind 0x02 is double precision for REAL (64-bit).
0265 Kind 0x04 is four-byte integer for INTEGER.
0266 Kind 0x08 is eight-byte integer for INTEGER.
0267 Bytes 2-3 of the type descriptor contain flags.
0268 Bit 15 of flags indicates whether the type is constant (1) or variable (0).
0269 Bit 14 of flags indicates whether the type is parameter (1) or not (0).
0270 Bit 13 of flags indicates whether the type is allocatable (1) or not (0).
0271 Bit 12 of flags indicates whether the type is pointer (1) or not (0).
0272 Bits 11-0 are reserved and must be zero.
0273 Bytes 4-7 of the type descriptor contain the byte size of one scalar element.
0274 For INTEGER(kind=1), the byte size is 4.
0275 For INTEGER(kind=2), the byte size is 2.
0276 For INTEGER(kind=4), the byte size is 4.
0277 For INTEGER(kind=8), the byte size is 8.
0278 For REAL(kind=4), the byte size is 4.
0279 For REAL(kind=8), the byte size is 8.
0280 For LOGICAL, the byte size is 1.
0281 For CHARACTER(len=N), the byte size is N.
0282 For CHARACTER(len=*), the byte size is zero until resolved.
0283 Bytes 8-11 of the type descriptor contain the array rank (0 for scalars).
0284 Bytes 12-15 of the type descriptor contain an offset to array dimension info.
0285 Array dimension info is a sequence of 16-byte dimension descriptors.
0286 Each dimension descriptor contains lower bound (4 bytes), upper bound (4 bytes).
0287 Each dimension descriptor contains stride (4 bytes) and extent (4 bytes).
0288 The extent is computed as max(0, upper_bound - lower_bound + 1).
0289 A zero-extent dimension produces a zero-size array.
0290 The total array size in bytes is element_size * product of all extents.
0291 Array size exceeding 2^32 bytes requires the extended word size for addressing.
0292 Implicit typing is disabled by default (IMPLICIT NONE semantics).
0293 If the source contains IMPLICIT NONE, the implicit typing flag is set to 0x00.
0294 If the source contains an IMPLICIT statement, the implicit typing flag is set to 0x01.
0295 The implicit typing flag is stored in the compilation unit header.
0296 The compilation unit header is 64 bytes.
0297 Bytes 0-3 of the compilation unit header contain the unit type.
0298 Unit type 0x01 is UT_PROGRAM.
0299 Unit type 0x02 is UT_SUBROUTINE.
0300 Unit type 0x03 is UT_FUNCTION.
0301 Unit type 0x04 is UT_BLOCK_DATA.
0302 Unit type 0x05 is UT_MODULE.
0303 Bytes 4-7 contain the unit name length.
0304 Bytes 8-11 contain the unit name offset in the string table.
0305 Bytes 12-15 contain the number of symbols in the symbol table.
0306 Bytes 16-19 contain the symbol table offset.
0307 Bytes 20-23 contain the number of statements in the statement list.
0308 Bytes 24-27 contain the statement list offset.
0309 Bytes 28-31 contain the implicit typing flag.
0310 Bytes 32-35 contain the number of common blocks.
0311 Bytes 36-39 contain the common block table offset.
0312 Bytes 40-43 contain the number of equivalence groups.
0313 Bytes 44-47 contain the equivalence table offset.
0314 Bytes 48-51 contain the number of data statements.
0315 Bytes 52-55 contain the data statement table offset.
0316 Bytes 56-59 contain the number of entry points.
0317 Bytes 60-63 contain the entry point table offset.
0318 The symbol table is an array of 32-byte symbol records.
0319 Bytes 0-3 of a symbol record contain the symbol name hash (32-bit FNV-1a).
0320 The FNV-1a offset basis is 0x811C9DC5.
0321 The FNV-1a prime is 0x01000193.
0322 Bytes 4-7 contain the symbol name offset in the string table.
0323 Bytes 8-11 contain the symbol name length.
0324 Bytes 12-15 contain the type descriptor offset.
0325 Bytes 16-19 contain the storage class.
0326 Storage class 0x01 is SC_LOCAL.
0327 Storage class 0x02 is SC_GLOBAL.
0328 Storage class 0x03 is SC_ARGUMENT.
0329 Storage class 0x04 is SC_COMMON.
0330 Storage class 0x05 is SC_EQUIVALENCE.
0331 Storage class 0x06 is SC_PARAMETER.
0332 Storage class 0x07 is SC_SAVE.
0333 Bytes 20-23 contain the initial value offset (zero if none).
0334 Bytes 24-27 contain the initial value size in bytes (zero if none).
0335 Bytes 28-31 contain symbol flags.
0336 Bit 31 of symbol flags indicates whether the symbol is used (1) or unused (0).
0337 Bit 30 of symbol flags indicates whether the symbol is assigned (1) or not (0).
0338 Bit 29 of symbol flags indicates whether the symbol is declared (1) or implicit (0).
0339 Bit 28 of symbol flags indicates whether the symbol is a function result (1) or not (0).
0340 Bits 27-0 are reserved and must be zero.
0341 The string table is a contiguous sequence of bytes with no delimiters.
0342 String offsets are zero-based from the start of the string table.
0343 String lengths are explicit; there are no null terminators.
0344 The string table follows the compilation unit header in the PTM-IR file.
0345 Symbol names are stored in uppercase in the string table.
0346 The statement list is an array of 16-byte statement records.
0347 Bytes 0-3 of a statement record contain the statement type.
0348 Statement type 0x01 is ST_ASSIGNMENT.
0349 Statement type 0x02 is ST_IF.
0350 Statement type 0x03 is ST_DO.
0351 Statement type 0x04 is ST_DO_WHILE.
0352 Statement type 0x05 is ST_CALL.
0353 Statement type 0x06 is ST_RETURN.
0354 Statement type 0x07 is ST_GOTO.
0355 Statement type 0x08 is ST_COMPUTED_GOTO.
0356 Statement type 0x09 is ST_ASSIGNED_GOTO.
0357 Statement type 0x0A is ST_STOP.
0358 Statement type 0x0B is ST_PAUSE.
0359 Statement type 0x0C is ST_READ.
0360 Statement type 0x0D is ST_WRITE.
0361 Statement type 0x0E is ST_PRINT.
0362 Statement type 0x0F is ST_OPEN.
0363 Statement type 0x10 is ST_CLOSE.
0364 Statement type 0x11 is ST_INQUIRE.
0365 Statement type 0x12 is ST_BACKSPACE.
0366 Statement type 0x13 is ST_REWIND.
0367 Statement type 0x14 is ST_ENDFILE.
0368 Statement type 0x15 is ST_FORMAT.
0369 Statement type 0x16 is ST_ENTRY.
0370 Statement type 0x17 is ST_DATA.
0371 Statement type 0x18 is ST_CONTINUE.
0372 Statement type 0x19 is ST_LABEL_ASSIGN.
0373 Statement type 0x1A is ST_BLOCK_IF.
0374 Statement type 0x1B is ST_ELSE.
0375 Statement type 0x1C is ST_ELSEIF.
0376 Statement type 0x1D is ST_ENDIF.
0377 Statement type 0x1E is ST_ENDDO.
0378 Statement type 0x1F is ST_SELECT_CASE.
0379 Statement type 0x20 is ST_CASE.
0380 Statement type 0x21 is ST_END_SELECT.
0381 Statement type 0x22 is ST_WHERE.
0382 Statement type 0x23 is ST_END_WHERE.
0383 Statement type 0x24 is ST_FORALL.
0384 Statement type 0x25 is ST_END_FORALL.
0385 Statement type 0x26 is ST_CYCLE.
0386 Statement type 0x27 is ST_EXIT.
0387 Statement type 0x28 is ST_ALLOCATE.
0388 Statement type 0x29 is ST_DEALLOCATE.
0389 Statement type 0x2A is ST_NULLIFY.
0390 Statement type 0x2B is ST_POINTER_ASSIGNMENT.
0391 Statement type 0x2C is ST_ARITHMETIC_IF.
0392 Statement type 0x2D is ST_LOGICAL_IF.
0393 Statement type 0x2E is ST_PROGRAM.
0394 Statement type 0x2F is ST_SUBROUTINE.
0395 Statement type 0x30 is ST_FUNCTION.
0396 Statement type 0x31 is ST_BLOCK_DATA.
0397 Statement type 0x32 is ST_MODULE.
0398 Statement type 0x33 is ST_USE.
0399 Statement type 0x34 is ST_INTERFACE.
0400 Statement type 0x35 is ST_END_INTERFACE.
0401 Statement type 0x36 is ST_CONTAINS.
0402 Statement type 0x37 is ST_IMPLICIT.
0403 Statement type 0x38 is ST_IMPLICIT_NONE.
0404 Statement type 0x39 is ST_TYPE_DECLARATION.
0405 Statement type 0x3A is ST_DIMENSION.
0406 Statement type 0x3B is ST_COMMON.
0407 Statement type 0x3C is ST_EQUIVALENCE.
0408 Statement type 0x3D is ST_PARAMETER.
0409 Statement type 0x3E is ST_EXTERNAL.
0410 Statement type 0x3F is ST_INTRINSIC.
0411 Statement type 0x40 is ST_SAVE.
0412 Statement type 0x41 is ST_INTENT.
0413 Statement type 0x42 is ST_OPTIONAL.
0414 Statement type 0x43 is ST_PUBLIC.
0415 Statement type 0x44 is ST_PRIVATE.
0416 Statement type 0x45 is ST_SEQUENCE.
0417 Statement type 0x46 is ST_DERIVED_TYPE.
0418 Statement type 0x47 is ST_END_TYPE.
0419 Statement type 0x48 is ST_NAMELIST.
0420 Statement type 0x49 is ST_STMT_FUNCTION.
0421 Statement type 0x4A is ST_HALT.
0422 Bytes 4-7 of a statement record contain the statement label (0 if unlabeled).
0423 Bytes 8-11 contain the line number of the source statement.
0424 Bytes 12-15 contain the offset to the statement detail structure.
0425 The statement detail structure is type-specific and follows the statement list.
0426 An assignment statement detail contains: 4 bytes lhs expression offset, 4 bytes rhs expression offset.
0427 An IF statement detail contains: 4 bytes condition expression offset, 4 bytes then-branch offset, 4 bytes else-branch offset.
0428 A DO statement detail contains: 4 bytes loop variable symbol index, 4 bytes initial value expression offset, 4 bytes final value expression offset, 4 bytes step expression offset.
0429 A CALL statement detail contains: 4 bytes callee symbol index, 4 bytes argument count, 4 bytes argument list offset.
0430 A RETURN statement detail contains: 4 bytes return value expression offset (0 for subroutine).
0431 A GOTO statement detail contains: 4 bytes target label value.
0432 Expression trees are encoded as a post-order sequence of nodes.
0433 Each expression node is 16 bytes.
0434 Bytes 0-3 contain the node type.
0435 Node type 0x01 is EXPR_VARIABLE.
0436 Node type 0x02 is EXPR_CONSTANT.
0437 Node type 0x03 is EXPR_UNARY_OP.
0438 Node type 0x04 is EXPR_BINARY_OP.
0439 Node type 0x05 is EXPR_TERNARY_OP.
0440 Node type 0x06 is EXPR_ARRAY_REF.
0441 Node type 0x07 is EXPR_FUNCTION_CALL.
0442 Node type 0x08 is EXPR_SUBSTRING.
0443 Node type 0x09 is EXPR_IMPLICIT_DO.
0444 Node type 0x0A is EXPR_STRUCTURE_CONSTRUCTOR.
0445 Bytes 4-7 contain the type descriptor offset for the result type.
0446 Bytes 8-11 contain operand A offset (or symbol index for variables).
0447 Bytes 12-15 contain operand B offset (or operator subtype for unary/binary ops).
0448 For EXPR_CONSTANT, bytes 8-11 contain the constant pool offset.
0449 For EXPR_CONSTANT, bytes 12-15 contain the constant size in bytes.
0450 The constant pool is a contiguous byte array following the expression nodes.
0451 Integer constants in the pool are stored in two's complement, big-endian.
0452 Real constants in the pool are stored in IEEE 754 binary64, big-endian.
0453 Logical constants in the pool are single bytes: 0x00 or 0x01.
0454 Character constants in the pool are raw ASCII bytes without delimiters.
0455 The expression tree is validated for type consistency before lowering.
0456 Type consistency requires that every operator's operands match the operator's signature.
0457 Assignment requires that the rhs type is assignment-compatible with the lhs type.
0458 INTEGER is assignment-compatible with INTEGER of any kind.
0459 REAL is assignment-compatible with REAL of any kind and with INTEGER.
0460 LOGICAL is assignment-compatible only with LOGICAL.
0461 CHARACTER is assignment-compatible only with CHARACTER of the same length or with length mismatch warning.
0462 Array assignment requires matching ranks and extents.
0463 Implicit type conversion from INTEGER to REAL is permitted in expressions.
0464 Implicit type conversion from REAL to INTEGER requires explicit truncation or rounding.
0465 Mixed-type arithmetic promotes INTEGER to REAL before operation.
0466 Mixed-kind arithmetic promotes to the higher precision kind.
0467 Type mismatches that are not permitted trigger a validation error.
0468 The type system validation is fail-closed.
0469 No type coercion is performed silently.
0470 Every type descriptor is hashable for deduplication.
0471 The type descriptor hash is computed over the 16-byte descriptor.
0472 Identical type descriptors share the same hash and the same offset.
0473 The type table is sorted by hash for binary search.
0474 Type table collisions are resolved by byte-by-byte comparison.
0475 The type table is validated for uniqueness before use.
0476 The symbol table is validated for name uniqueness within each scope.
0477 Duplicate symbol names in the same scope trigger a validation error.
0478 Scope nesting is tracked by a scope stack encoded in the symbol flags.
0479 Scope depth is limited to 256 levels.
0480 Exceeding 256 scope levels triggers a validation failure.
0481 The scope stack is encoded as a linked list in the symbol table.
0482 Each scope record is 8 bytes: 4 bytes parent scope index, 4 bytes symbol count.
0483 Scope index 0xFFFFFFFF indicates the global scope (no parent).
0484 The global scope always has index zero.
0485 Program units introduce new scopes.
0486 BLOCK constructs introduce new scopes.
0487 DO constructs with local variables introduce new scopes.
0488 IF constructs do not introduce new scopes for variables.
0489 Statement functions introduce new scopes.
0490 Internal procedures introduce new scopes.
0491 Module procedures introduce new scopes.
0492 Interface blocks introduce new scopes.
0493 Derived type definitions introduce new scopes.
0494 The type system binary representation is the foundation for all subsequent lowering.
0495 All offsets in the type system are relative to the start of the PTM-IR payload.
0496 The PTM-IR payload offset base is zero immediately after the 16-byte file header.
0497 The PTM-IR file structure is: header (16 bytes), compilation unit header (64 bytes), string table, symbol table, type table, statement list, statement details, expression nodes, constant pool, scope table.
0498 Each section is aligned to an 8-byte boundary.
0499 Padding between sections consists of zero bytes.
0500 The total PTM-IR file size is recorded in the header payload length field.

SECTION 0003: CONTROL FLOW GRAPH BINARY ENCODING
================================================================================

0501 The control flow graph (CFG) is derived from the statement list.
0502 The CFG is a directed graph where nodes are basic blocks.
0503 A basic block is a maximal sequence of consecutive statements with a single entry and single exit.
0504 The entry point of a basic block is the first statement in the sequence.
0505 The exit point of a basic block is the last statement in the sequence.
0506 A basic block terminates at a branch, a conditional, a call, a return, or a label target.
0507 The CFG is encoded as a binary file with magic 0x43464742 ('CFGB').
0508 The CFG file header is 16 bytes standard header plus 32 bytes of CFG-specific metadata.
0509 Bytes 16-19 of the CFG header contain the number of basic blocks (N).
0510 Bytes 20-23 contain the number of edges (E).
0511 Bytes 24-27 contain the entry block index (0-based).
0512 Bytes 28-31 contain the exit block index (0-based, 0xFFFFFFFF if multiple exits).
0513 Bytes 32-35 contain the number of loop headers.
0514 Bytes 36-39 contain the loop header table offset.
0515 Bytes 40-43 contain the dominator tree offset.
0516 Bytes 44-47 contain the post-dominator tree offset.
0517 The basic block table follows the header and contains N records of 32 bytes each.
0518 Bytes 0-3 of a basic block record contain the block index.
0519 Bytes 4-7 contain the first statement index (into the statement list).
0520 Bytes 8-11 contain the last statement index (inclusive).
0521 Bytes 12-15 contain the number of predecessor edges.
0522 Bytes 16-19 contain the predecessor edge list offset.
0523 Bytes 20-23 contain the number of successor edges.
0524 Bytes 24-27 contain the successor edge list offset.
0525 Bytes 28-31 contain block flags.
0526 Bit 31 of block flags indicates whether the block is a loop header (1) or not (0).
0527 Bit 30 of block flags indicates whether the block is a loop exit (1) or not (0).
0528 Bit 29 of block flags indicates whether the block is unreachable (1) or reachable (0).
0529 Bit 28 of block flags indicates whether the block contains a call (1) or not (0).
0530 Bit 27 of block flags indicates whether the block contains an I/O operation (1) or not (0).
0531 Bits 26-0 are reserved and must be zero.
0532 The edge table contains E records of 16 bytes each.
0533 Bytes 0-3 of an edge record contain the source block index.
0534 Bytes 4-7 contain the target block index.
0535 Bytes 8-11 contain the edge type.
0536 Edge type 0x01 is EDGE_FALLTHROUGH.
0537 Edge type 0x02 is EDGE_BRANCH_TRUE.
0538 Edge type 0x03 is EDGE_BRANCH_FALSE.
0539 Edge type 0x04 is EDGE_UNCONDITIONAL.
0540 Edge type 0x05 is EDGE_CALL.
0541 Edge type 0x06 is EDGE_RETURN.
0542 Edge type 0x07 is EDGE_EXCEPTION.
0543 Edge type 0x08 is EDGE_BACK_EDGE.
0544 Bytes 12-15 of an edge record contain edge flags.
0545 Bit 31 of edge flags indicates whether the edge is critical (1) or not (0).
0546 A critical edge is an edge from a block with multiple successors to a block with multiple predecessors.
0547 Critical edges are split by inserting empty blocks during optimization.
0548 The dominator tree is encoded as an array of N 32-bit integers.
0549 Dominator tree entry i contains the index of the immediate dominator of block i.
0550 The entry block dominates itself; its immediate dominator is itself.
0551 A block with no dominator (unreachable) has dominator index 0xFFFFFFFF.
0552 The post-dominator tree is encoded similarly with N 32-bit integers.
0553 Post-dominator entry i contains the index of the immediate post-dominator of block i.
0554 The loop header table contains one 16-byte record per loop.
0555 Bytes 0-3 contain the header block index.
0556 Bytes 4-7 contain the number of blocks in the loop body.
0557 Bytes 8-11 contain the loop body block list offset.
0558 Bytes 12-15 contain the nesting depth (0 for outermost loops).
0559 Loop nesting depth is limited to 64 levels.
0560 Exceeding 64 loop nesting levels triggers a validation failure.
0561 The loop body block list is an array of 32-bit block indices.
0562 Natural loops are identified by back edges in the CFG.
0563 A back edge is an edge whose target dominates its source.
0564 The natural loop of a back edge (h -> s) consists of all nodes that can reach s without passing through h.
0565 The loop detection algorithm runs in O(N + E) time.
0566 The dominator tree is computed using the Lengauer-Tarjan algorithm in O(E * alpha(E, N)) time.
0567 The post-dominator tree is computed by reversing all edges and applying the same algorithm.
0568 Reachability is computed by depth-first search from the entry block.
0569 Unreachable blocks are marked with the unreachable flag.
0570 Unreachable code does not participate in Turing Machine lowering.
0571 Unreachable code is reported as a validation warning.
0572 The CFG is validated before lowering to the Turing Machine model.
0573 CFG validation checks that every block index is within range [0, N-1].
0574 CFG validation checks that every edge references valid block indices.
0575 CFG validation checks that the entry block has no predecessors.
0576 CFG validation checks that the exit block (if unique) has no successors.
0577 CFG validation checks that the dominator tree is acyclic.
0578 CFG validation checks that the post-dominator tree is acyclic.
0579 CFG validation checks that loop headers have at least two predecessors.
0580 CFG validation checks that back edges target loop headers.
0581 CFG validation checks that every block is reachable from the entry or is explicitly marked unreachable.
0582 Any CFG validation failure stops the pipeline with fail-closed status.
0583 The CFG file hash is SHA-256 over the complete CFG file.
0584 The CFG hash is recorded in the audit manifest.
0585 The CFG is constructed from the PTM-IR statement list without modifying the PTM-IR file.
0586 The CFG construction reads the PTM-IR file as read-only input.
0587 The CFG construction does not execute arbitrary commands.
0588 The CFG construction does not access network resources.
0589 The CFG construction does not write outside the vault boundary.
0590 The CFG construction treats the PTM-IR file as untrusted input.
0591 The CFG construction validates the PTM-IR header before processing.
0592 The CFG construction validates the statement list bounds before indexing.
0593 The CFG construction validates the symbol table bounds before symbol lookup.
0594 The CFG construction validates the expression node bounds before tree traversal.
0595 The CFG construction validates the constant pool bounds before constant access.
0596 All bounds checks use unsigned comparison to prevent wrap-around exploits.
0597 Array indexing uses base pointer plus offset with explicit length checking.
0598 Pointer arithmetic is not used for structure traversal.
0599 Structure offsets are compile-time constants defined in this specification.
0600 The CFG binary encoding is deterministic: identical source produces identical CFG bytes.

SECTION 0004: POLYNOMIAL TURING MACHINE BINARY SPECIFICATION
================================================================================

0601 The Polynomial Turing Machine (PTM) is a multi-tape deterministic Turing machine.
0602 The PTM operates in time polynomial in the length of its encoded input.
0603 The PTM has exactly four tapes: input tape, work tape, control tape, output tape.
0604 Each tape is a one-way infinite sequence of cells extending to the right.
0605 Each cell holds exactly one symbol from the tape alphabet.
0606 The tape alphabet for the input tape is {0, 1, B}.
0607 The tape alphabet for the work tape is {0, 1, B, X, Y, Z}.
0608 The tape alphabet for the control tape is {0, 1, B}.
0609 The tape alphabet for the output tape is {0, 1, B}.
0610 The blank symbol B is distinct from 0 and 1.
0611 The input tape is read-only after initialization.
0612 The output tape is write-only.
0613 The work tape and control tape are read-write.
0614 Each tape has its own read-write head.
0615 All heads start at cell index 0.
0616 The input tape head can move right (R) or stay (S) but not left.
0617 The work tape head can move left (L), right (R), or stay (S).
0618 The control tape head can move left (L), right (R), or stay (S).
0619 The output tape head can move right (R) or stay (S) but not left.
0620 The finite control is encoded as a binary transition table.
0621 The transition table file uses magic 0x544D5454 ('TMTT').
0622 The transition table header is 16 bytes standard plus 48 bytes of PTM metadata.
0623 Bytes 16-19 contain the number of states (Q).
0624 Bytes 20-23 contain the number of transitions (T).
0625 Bytes 24-27 contain the initial state index (0-based).
0626 Bytes 28-31 contain the accepting state index.
0627 Bytes 32-35 contain the rejecting state index.
0628 Bytes 36-39 contain the halting state index.
0629 Bytes 40-43 contain the input alphabet size (always 3 for {0, 1, B}).
0630 Bytes 44-47 contain the work alphabet size (always 6 for {0, 1, B, X, Y, Z}).
0631 Bytes 48-51 contain the control alphabet size (always 3).
0632 Bytes 52-55 contain the output alphabet size (always 3).
0633 Bytes 56-59 contain the maximum number of tapes (always 4).
0634 Bytes 60-63 contain the polynomial degree bound (d).
0635 The polynomial degree bound d is a 32-bit unsigned integer.
0636 The time bound is T(n) <= c * n^d for some constant c and all n >= n0.
0637 The space bound is S(n) <= c' * n^d for some constant c' and all n >= n0.
0638 The constants c and c' are recorded in the complexity report.
0639 The complexity report is a separate artifact with magic 0x434D504C ('CMPL').
0640 The state table follows the header and contains Q records of 16 bytes each.
0641 Bytes 0-3 of a state record contain the state index.
0642 Bytes 4-7 contain the state type.
0643 State type 0x01 is STATE_NORMAL.
0644 State type 0x02 is STATE_INITIAL.
0645 State type 0x03 is STATE_ACCEPTING.
0646 State type 0x04 is STATE_REJECTING.
0647 State type 0x05 is STATE_HALTING.
0648 Bytes 8-11 contain the number of outgoing transitions from this state.
0649 Bytes 12-15 contain the offset to the outgoing transition list.
0650 The transition table contains T records of 48 bytes each.
0651 Bytes 0-3 contain the transition identifier (unique 32-bit integer).
0652 Bytes 4-7 contain the source state index.
0653 Bytes 8-11 contain the target state index.
0654 Bytes 12-15 contain the input tape symbol read (0, 1, or B encoded as 0x00, 0x01, 0x02).
0655 Bytes 16-19 contain the work tape symbol read (0x00-0x05).
0656 Bytes 20-23 contain the control tape symbol read (0x00-0x02).
0657 Bytes 24-27 contain the output tape symbol read (0x00-0x02).
0658 Bytes 28-31 contain the input tape symbol written (0x00-0x02).
0659 Bytes 32-35 contain the work tape symbol written (0x00-0x05).
0660 Bytes 36-39 contain the control tape symbol written (0x00-0x02).
0661 Bytes 40-43 contain the output tape symbol written (0x00-0x02).
0662 Bytes 44 contain the input tape head movement: 0x00=L, 0x01=R, 0x02=S.
0663 Bytes 45 contain the work tape head movement: 0x00=L, 0x01=R, 0x02=S.
0664 Bytes 46 contain the control tape head movement: 0x00=L, 0x01=R, 0x02=S.
0665 Bytes 47 contain the output tape head movement: 0x00=L, 0x01=R, 0x02=S.
0666 The input tape head movement L (left) is prohibited and must not appear.
0667 The output tape head movement L (left) is prohibited and must not appear.
0668 Any transition with prohibited head movement triggers a validation failure.
0669 The transition table is deterministic: for each state and tape symbol combination, at most one transition applies.
0670 Determinism is verified by checking that no two transitions from the same state have identical read symbols on all tapes.
0671 The determinism check runs in O(T log T) time by sorting transitions on (source_state, read_symbols).
0672 The initial state has exactly one outgoing transition for each possible input tape symbol.
0673 The accepting state has no outgoing transitions.
0674 The rejecting state has no outgoing transitions.
0675 The halting state has no outgoing transitions.
0676 Every normal state must have at least one outgoing transition.
0677 A state with no outgoing transitions and not designated as accepting/rejecting/halting triggers a validation warning.
0678 The tape alphabet encoding is: 0x00 -> symbol 0, 0x01 -> symbol 1, 0x02 -> symbol B.
0679 The work tape alphabet encoding is: 0x00 -> 0, 0x01 -> 1, 0x02 -> B, 0x03 -> X, 0x04 -> Y, 0x05 -> Z.
0680 The symbols X, Y, Z on the work tape are markers for algorithm-specific purposes.
0681 X marks the left boundary of a data region.
0682 Y marks the right boundary of a data region.
0683 Z marks a processed or visited cell.
0684 The input tape is initialized with the binary encoding of the PTM-IR program.
0685 The binary encoding of the PTM-IR program is the raw bytes of the PTM-IR file.
0686 The input tape cells hold the bits of the PTM-IR file in sequence.
0687 Each bit is encoded as symbol 0 or symbol 1 on the input tape.
0688 After the last bit of the input, the input tape contains blank symbols B.
0689 The work tape is initialized with all blank symbols B.
0690 The control tape is initialized with the binary encoding of the initial control state.
0691 The output tape is initialized with all blank symbols B.
0692 A configuration of the PTM is a 5-tuple: (state, input_head, work_head, control_head, output_head, tape_contents).
0693 The configuration is encoded as a binary snapshot for debugging and audit.
0694 The snapshot format uses magic 0x534E4150 ('SNAP').
0695 The snapshot header is 16 bytes standard plus 32 bytes of snapshot metadata.
0696 Bytes 16-19 contain the step number (0-based).
0697 Bytes 20-23 contain the current state index.
0698 Bytes 24-27 contain the input head position.
0699 Bytes 28-31 contain the work head position.
0700 Bytes 32-35 contain the control head position.
0701 Bytes 36-39 contain the output head position.
0702 Bytes 40-43 contain the work tape content length in cells.
0703 Bytes 44-47 contain the control tape content length in cells.
0704 The tape contents follow as sequences of byte-encoded symbols.
0705 Snapshots are generated at configurable intervals for audit purposes.
0706 Snapshot generation does not affect the deterministic execution of the PTM.
0707 The PTM simulation halts when it enters the accepting, rejecting, or halting state.
0708 The PTM simulation also halts when the step count exceeds the polynomial bound.
0709 Exceeding the polynomial bound is treated as a rejection.
0710 The polynomial bound is computed as c * n^d where n is the input bit length.
0711 The input bit length n is the number of bits in the PTM-IR file multiplied by eight.
0712 Wait: the input bit length n is the number of bits in the PTM-IR file, which is 8 times the byte length.
0713 The constant c is determined by the complexity analysis phase.
0714 The constant d is the polynomial degree recorded in the transition table header.
0715 For the canonical sum-to-100 example, n is the bit length of the encoded PTM-IR.
0716 The loop bound 100 is a constant, not part of the input encoding.
0717 Therefore the canonical example runs in O(1) time with respect to the input size.
0718 The complexity report must state that the canonical example has constant time complexity.
0719 The complexity report must not claim polynomial time merely because the source contains a loop.
0720 The complexity report must distinguish source-level constants from input-dependent variables.
0721 The complexity report must state the encoding explicitly: binary encoding of PTM-IR.
0722 The complexity report must state the measured values of c and d.
0723 If d cannot be determined, the complexity report must label it UNPROVEN.
0724 If c cannot be determined, the complexity report must label it UNPROVEN.
0725 The complexity report is validated for consistency with the transition table.
0726 The complexity report validation checks that d matches the header field.
0727 The complexity report validation checks that c is a positive finite number.
0728 The complexity report validation checks that n0 is a positive integer.
0729 The complexity report file structure is: header (16 bytes), 4 bytes for d, 8 bytes for c (IEEE 754 binary64), 4 bytes for n0, 256 bytes for complexity proof text.
0730 The complexity proof text is ASCII, padded with spaces to 256 bytes.
0731 The complexity proof text must reference the specific encoding and bound derivation.
0732 The PTM transition table is validated before simulation.
0733 PTM validation checks magic, version, header checksum, and payload length.
0734 PTM validation checks that state indices are within [0, Q-1].
0735 PTM validation checks that transition source and target states are valid.
0736 PTM validation checks that read and write symbols are within alphabet sizes.
0737 PTM validation checks that head movements are valid for each tape.
0738 PTM validation checks determinism by sorting and scanning transitions.
0739 PTM validation checks that initial, accepting, rejecting, and halting states are distinct.
0740 PTM validation checks that every state is reachable from the initial state.
0741 Reachability is computed by BFS over the transition graph.
0742 Unreachable states are reported as warnings but do not fail validation.
0743 Dead states (reachable but no path to accepting/rejecting/halting) are reported as warnings.
0744 The PTM validation produces a validation report with magic 0x56414C52 ('VALR').
0745 The validation report contains one 32-byte record per check.
0746 Each record contains: 4 bytes check ID, 4 bytes status (0=PASS, 1=FAIL, 2=WARN), 24 bytes message.
0747 A FAIL status stops the pipeline.
0748 A WARN status is recorded in the audit trail but does not stop the pipeline.
0749 The PTM file hash is SHA-256 over the complete transition table file.
0750 The PTM file hash is recorded in the audit manifest.

SECTION 0005: QUIPPER CIRCUIT BINARY REPRESENTATION
================================================================================

0751 The quantum layer uses Quipper as the circuit representation format.
0752 Quipper is a functional language for describing quantum circuits.
0753 The binary encoding of Quipper artifacts is specified in this section.
0754 The Quipper artifact file uses magic 0x51554950 ('QUIP').
0755 The Quipper file header is 16 bytes standard plus 64 bytes of Quipper metadata.
0756 Bytes 16-19 contain the number of qubits (Q).
0757 Bytes 20-23 contain the number of classical bits (C).
0758 Bytes 24-27 contain the number of circuit gates (G).
0759 Bytes 28-31 contain the number of subroutines (S).
0760 Bytes 32-35 contain the number of wire declarations (W).
0761 Bytes 36-39 contain the qubit register table offset.
0762 Bytes 40-43 contain the classical register table offset.
0763 Bytes 44-47 contain the gate sequence offset.
0764 Bytes 48-51 contain the subroutine table offset.
0765 Bytes 52-55 contain the wire table offset.
0766 Bytes 56-59 contain the circuit depth.
0767 Bytes 60-63 contain the total gate count including subroutines.
0768 A qubit is a two-level quantum system with basis states |0> and |1>.
0769 A classical bit is a two-level classical system with values 0 and 1.
0770 A wire represents the evolution of a qubit or classical bit through time.
0771 The qubit register table contains Q records of 16 bytes each.
0772 Bytes 0-3 contain the register index.
0773 Bytes 4-7 contain the register name offset in the string table.
0774 Bytes 8-11 contain the register name length.
0775 Bytes 12-15 contain the number of qubits in the register.
0776 The classical register table contains C records of 16 bytes each with identical layout.
0777 The wire table contains W records of 8 bytes each.
0778 Bytes 0-3 contain the wire index.
0779 Bytes 4 contain the wire type: 0x01 for quantum, 0x02 for classical.
0780 Bytes 5 contain the wire direction: 0x01 for input, 0x02 for output, 0x03 for internal.
0781 Bytes 6-7 contain the register index that owns this wire.
0782 The gate sequence is an array of G gate records of 32 bytes each.
0783 Bytes 0-3 contain the gate index (0-based, monotonic).
0784 Bytes 4-7 contain the gate type identifier.
0785 Gate type 0x01 is GATE_H (Hadamard).
0786 Gate type 0x02 is GATE_X (Pauli-X).
0787 Gate type 0x03 is GATE_Y (Pauli-Y).
0788 Gate type 0x04 is GATE_Z (Pauli-Z).
0789 Gate type 0x05 is GATE_S (Phase).
0790 Gate type 0x06 is GATE_T (T gate, pi/8).
0791 Gate type 0x07 is GATE_S_DAG (S dagger, inverse of S).
0792 Gate type 0x08 is GATE_T_DAG (T dagger, inverse of T).
0793 Gate type 0x09 is GATE_CNOT (Controlled-NOT).
0794 Gate type 0x0A is GATE_CZ (Controlled-Z).
0795 Gate type 0x0B is GATE_SWAP (Swap).
0796 Gate type 0x0C is GATE_TOFFOLI (CCNOT, Toffoli).
0797 Gate type 0x0D is GATE_FREDKIN (CSWAP, Fredkin).
0798 Gate type 0x0E is GATE_MEASURE (Measurement in computational basis).
0799 Gate type 0x0F is GATE_RESET (Reset to |0>).
0800 Gate type 0x10 is GATE_INIT (Initialize to specified state).
0801 Gate type 0x11 is GATE_RZ (Rotation around Z axis by angle theta).
0802 Gate type 0x12 is GATE_RX (Rotation around X axis by angle theta).
0803 Gate type 0x13 is GATE_RY (Rotation around Y axis by angle theta).
0804 Gate type 0x14 is GATE_PHASE (Global phase rotation).
0805 Gate type 0x15 is GATE_U1 (Universal single-qubit rotation U1).
0806 Gate type 0x16 is GATE_U2 (Universal single-qubit rotation U2).
0807 Gate type 0x17 is GATE_U3 (Universal single-qubit rotation U3).
0808 Gate type 0x18 is GATE_CUSTOM (User-defined unitary).
0809 Gate type 0x19 is GATE_SUBROUTINE_CALL (Invocation of a named subroutine).
0810 Gate type 0x1A is GATE_COMMENT (Non-executable annotation).
0811 Gate type 0x1B is GATE_BARRIER (Synchronization barrier).
0812 Gate type 0x1C is GATE_CLASSICAL_NOT (Classical NOT).
0813 Gate type 0x1D is GATE_CLASSICAL_AND (Classical AND).
0814 Gate type 0x1E is GATE_CLASSICAL_OR (Classical OR).
0815 Gate type 0x1F is GATE_CLASSICAL_XOR (Classical XOR).
0816 Gate type 0x20 is GATE_CLASSICAL_COPY (Classical copy/fanout).
0817 Bytes 8-11 of a gate record contain the number of target qubits.
0818 Bytes 12-15 contain the target qubit list offset.
0819 Bytes 16-19 contain the number of control qubits.
0820 Bytes 20-23 contain the control qubit list offset.
0821 Bytes 24-27 contain the number of classical bits (for measurement or classical gates).
0822 Bytes 28-31 contain the classical bit list offset.
0823 The target qubit list is an array of 32-bit qubit indices.
0824 The control qubit list is an array of 32-bit qubit indices.
0825 The classical bit list is an array of 32-bit classical bit indices.
0826 Qubit indices are global and range from 0 to Q-1.
0827 Classical bit indices are global and range from 0 to C-1.
0828 A gate with zero targets and zero controls is a global operation or annotation.
0829 A measurement gate has at least one target qubit and at least one classical bit.
0830 A measurement gate maps each target qubit to a corresponding classical bit.
0831 The mapping is one-to-one: target qubit i maps to classical bit i in the list.
0832 A reset gate has at least one target qubit and no classical bits.
0833 A barrier gate has no targets and no controls; it applies to all wires in scope.
0834 A comment gate has no targets, no controls, and no classical bits.
0835 The comment text follows the gate record in the payload.
0836 Comment text length is specified in bytes 8-11 of the gate record (reused as text length).
0837 Rotation gates (RZ, RX, RY, PHASE, U1, U2, U3) include an angle parameter.
0838 The angle parameter is an IEEE 754 binary64 value following the gate record.
0839 For U2 and U3, multiple angle parameters follow in sequence: phi, lambda, theta.
0840 Custom unitary gates include a unitary matrix specification.
0841 The unitary matrix is encoded as a sequence of complex numbers.
0842 Each complex number is two IEEE 754 binary64 values: real part then imaginary part.
0843 The matrix dimension is 2^k by 2^k where k is the number of target qubits.
0844 The matrix entries are stored in row-major order.
0845 The matrix is validated to be unitary within floating-point tolerance.
0846 The tolerance for unitarity is 1e-10.
0847 A matrix failing the unitarity check triggers a validation failure.
0848 The subroutine table contains S records of 32 bytes each.
0849 Bytes 0-3 contain the subroutine index.
0850 Bytes 4-7 contain the subroutine name offset in the string table.
0851 Bytes 8-11 contain the subroutine name length.
0852 Bytes 12-15 contain the number of input qubits.
0853 Bytes 16-19 contain the number of output qubits.
0854 Bytes 20-23 contain the number of input classical bits.
0855 Bytes 24-27 contain the number of output classical bits.
0856 Bytes 28-31 contain the offset to the subroutine body gate sequence.
0857 A subroutine body is a nested gate sequence with its own local wire numbering.
0858 Subroutine calls in the main gate sequence reference the subroutine by index.
0859 Subroutine calls are validated against the subroutine signature.
0860 The number of actual parameters must match the number of formal parameters.
0861 The types of actual parameters (quantum/classical) must match the formal parameters.
0862 Recursive subroutine calls are prohibited.
0863 Recursion is detected by building a call graph and checking for cycles.
0864 A cyclic call graph triggers a validation failure.
0865 The circuit depth is the length of the longest path from input to output in the gate dependency graph.
0866 The gate dependency graph is a DAG where edges represent wire data dependencies.
0867 Two gates that operate on disjoint sets of qubits can execute in parallel.
0868 Parallel gates occupy the same depth level.
0869 The depth is computed by topological sort of the dependency DAG.
0870 The depth computation runs in O(G + E) where E is the number of dependency edges.
0871 The total gate count includes all gates in the main sequence and all subroutine bodies.
0872 The total gate count is validated against the header field.
0873 The Quipper artifact does not claim automatic polynomial speedup.
0874 The Quipper artifact states the computational problem being represented.
0875 The computational problem is stated in the problem description field of the header.
0876 Bytes 64-127 of the Quipper header contain the problem description (64 ASCII characters, space-padded).
0877 The problem description for the canonical example is 'SUMMATION OF INTEGERS 1 TO N'.
0878 The problem description must not exceed 64 characters.
0879 The Quipper artifact states any oracle assumptions explicitly.
0880 Oracle assumptions are recorded in the oracle descriptor table.
0881 The oracle descriptor table contains one record per oracle.
0882 Each oracle record is 32 bytes: 4 bytes oracle index, 4 bytes input qubits, 4 bytes output qubits, 20 bytes oracle name.
0883 An oracle is a black-box unitary whose internal structure is not specified.
0884 The circuit size is the pair (Q, G) where Q is qubit count and G is gate count.
0885 The circuit size is recorded in the complexity report.
0886 The circuit width is Q (number of qubits).
0887 The circuit volume is Q * depth.
0888 The circuit volume is recorded in the complexity report as an auxiliary metric.
0889 The Quipper artifact is validated before publication.
0890 Quipper validation checks magic, version, header checksum, and payload length.
0891 Quipper validation checks that qubit indices are within [0, Q-1].
0892 Quipper validation checks that classical bit indices are within [0, C-1].
0893 Quipper validation checks that gate types are within the defined enumeration.
0894 Quipper validation checks that measurement gates have matching target and classical bit counts.
0895 Quipper validation checks that unitary matrices satisfy the unitarity condition.
0896 Quipper validation checks that subroutine signatures match their call sites.
0897 Quipper validation checks that the call graph is acyclic.
0898 Quipper validation checks that wire declarations are consistent with gate usage.
0899 Quipper validation checks that every wire is used at least once (no dangling wires).
0900 Dangling wires are reported as warnings but do not fail validation.
0901 The Quipper file hash is SHA-256 over the complete Quipper artifact file.
0902 The Quipper file hash is recorded in the audit manifest.
0903 The Quipper artifact is separate from the classical PTM artifact.
0904 The Quipper artifact is an optional acceleration or verification layer.
0905 The classical PTM execution does not depend on the Quipper artifact.
0906 The Quipper artifact does not modify the classical PTM transition table.
0907 The Quipper artifact does not modify the PTM-IR file.
0908 The Quipper artifact does not access files outside the vault boundary.
0909 The Quipper artifact does not execute arbitrary commands.
0910 The Quipper artifact does not request or emit credentials.
0911 The Quipper artifact does not claim quantum supremacy without proof.
0912 The Quipper artifact does not use OpenQASM as the interchange format.
0913 OpenQASM translation, if performed, is a separate output artifact with distinct magic number.
0914 The OpenQASM translation artifact uses magic 0x4F51534D ('OQSM') and is not the primary output.
0915 The primary quantum output is the Quipper artifact with magic 0x51554950.
0916 The Quipper string table follows the same rules as the PTM-IR string table.
0917 The Quipper string table is a contiguous byte array with explicit offsets and lengths.
0918 The Quipper string table contains register names, subroutine names, and comment texts.
0919 The Quipper string table is aligned to an 8-byte boundary.
0920 The Quipper payload structure is: header (80 bytes), string table, qubit register table, classical register table, wire table, gate sequence, subroutine table, oracle descriptor table.
0921 Each section is aligned to an 8-byte boundary with zero padding.
0922 The Quipper artifact is deterministic: identical PTM-IR produces identical Quipper bytes.
0923 Determinism is verified by hash comparison across repeated runs.
0924 The Quipper artifact generation does not use randomness.
0925 Any pseudo-random values required for simulation use a fixed seed of zero.
0926 The fixed seed is recorded in the audit manifest.
0927 The Quipper artifact generation treats the PTM-IR as untrusted input.
0928 The Quipper artifact generation validates the PTM-IR header before processing.
0929 The Quipper artifact generation validates the CFG before circuit construction.
0930 The Quipper artifact generation validates the type system before qubit allocation.
0931 The Quipper artifact generation is fail-closed on any validation error.
0932 The Quipper artifact generation does not silently skip unsupported constructs.
0933 An unsupported construct triggers a validation failure with explicit error code.
0934 The error code is a 4-byte integer: 0x00000001 for unsupported type, 0x00000002 for unsupported control flow, etc.
0935 Error codes are documented in the error code table.
0936 The error code table is a vault asset with magic 0x45525254 ('ERRT').
0937 The error code table contains 256 records of 32 bytes each.
0938 Each record contains: 4 bytes error code, 28 bytes error description (ASCII, space-padded).
0939 The error code table is read-only and its hash is recorded in the audit manifest.
0940 The Quipper artifact generation writes no files outside the vault.
0941 The Quipper artifact generation reads no files outside the vault.
0942 The Quipper artifact generation does not access network resources.
0943 The Quipper artifact generation does not infer credentials.
0944 The Quipper artifact generation does not emit credentials.
0945 The Quipper artifact generation treats every generated artifact as reproducible.
0946 Reproducibility requires that all inputs, configurations, and seeds are recorded.
0947 The Quipper artifact generation records its own version in the audit manifest.
0948 The version is a 32-bit integer: major (8 bits), minor (8 bits), patch (16 bits).
0949 The current version is 0x01000000 (major=1, minor=0, patch=0).
0950 The Quipper artifact generation completes with a success code in the execution manifest.

SECTION 0006: JCL VAULT MANIFEST BINARY FORMAT
================================================================================

0951 The JCL Vault Manifest controls the sandboxed batch execution environment.
0952 The manifest file uses magic 0x4A434C4D ('JCLM').
0953 The manifest header is 16 bytes standard plus 128 bytes of JCL metadata.
0954 Bytes 16-19 contain the manifest version (0x00000001).
0955 Bytes 20-23 contain the number of job steps (J).
0956 Bytes 24-27 contain the number of datasets (D).
0957 Bytes 28-31 contain the number of environment variables (E).
0958 Bytes 32-35 contain the number of resource limits (R).
0959 Bytes 36-39 contain the number of security policies (P).
0960 Bytes 40-43 contain the job step table offset.
0961 Bytes 44-47 contain the dataset table offset.
0962 Bytes 48-51 contain the environment table offset.
0963 Bytes 52-55 contain the resource limit table offset.
0964 Bytes 56-59 contain the security policy table offset.
0965 Bytes 60-63 contain the vault root path offset in the string table.
0966 Bytes 64-67 contain the vault root path length.
0967 Bytes 68-71 contain the execution user ID (32-bit unsigned, 0 for default).
0968 Bytes 72-75 contain the execution group ID (32-bit unsigned, 0 for default).
0969 Bytes 76-79 contain the umask value (default 0x0000007F for read-only others).
0970 Bytes 80-83 contain the maximum file descriptor count.
0971 Bytes 84-87 contain the maximum memory limit in bytes.
0972 Bytes 88-91 contain the maximum CPU time limit in seconds.
0973 Bytes 92-95 contain the maximum wall clock time limit in seconds.
0974 Bytes 96-99 contain the maximum process count.
0975 Bytes 100-103 contain the maximum open file count.
0976 Bytes 104-107 contain the maximum stack size in bytes.
0977 Bytes 108-111 contain the maximum data segment size in bytes.
0978 Bytes 112-115 contain the maximum core file size in bytes.
0979 Bytes 116-119 contain the maximum resident set size in bytes.
0980 Bytes 120-123 contain the maximum message queue size in bytes.
0981 Bytes 124-127 contain the maximum semaphore count.
0982 Bytes 128-131 contain the maximum shared memory segment size in bytes.
0983 Bytes 132-135 contain the maximum number of signals.
0984 Bytes 136-139 contain the maximum number of timers.
0985 Bytes 140-143 contain the job priority (0-99, lower is higher priority).
0986 The job step table contains J records of 64 bytes each.
0987 Bytes 0-3 contain the step index.
0988 Bytes 4-7 contain the step name offset in the string table.
0989 Bytes 8-11 contain the step name length.
0990 Bytes 12-15 contain the program name offset in the string table.
0991 Bytes 16-19 contain the program name length.
0992 Bytes 20-23 contain the number of input datasets.
0993 Bytes 24-27 contain the input dataset list offset.
0994 Bytes 28-31 contain the number of output datasets.
0995 Bytes 32-35 contain the output dataset list offset.
0996 Bytes 36-39 contain the condition code for step success (default 0).
0997 Bytes 40-43 contain the condition code for step failure (default 8).
0998 Bytes 44-47 contain the step dependency mask.
0999 Bit 31 of the dependency mask indicates whether the step depends on the previous step (1) or not (0).
1000 Bit 30 of the dependency mask indicates whether the step is allowed to run in parallel (1) or not (0).
1001 Bits 29-0 are reserved and must be zero.
1002 Bytes 48-51 contain the step timeout in seconds (0 for no timeout).
1003 Bytes 52-55 contain the step memory limit in bytes (0 for inherited limit).
1004 Bytes 56-59 contain the step CPU limit in seconds (0 for inherited limit).
1005 Bytes 60-63 contain step flags.
1006 Bit 31 of step flags indicates whether the step is critical (1) or optional (0).
1007 A critical step failure aborts the entire job.
1008 An optional step failure is logged but does not abort the job.
1009 Bit 30 of step flags indicates whether the step requires network access (1) or not (0).
1010 Network access is denied by default; explicit permission is required.
1011 Bit 29 of step flags indicates whether the step requires file system access outside the vault (1) or not (0).
1012 File system access outside the vault is denied by default.
1013 Bit 28 of step flags indicates whether the step is allowed to execute subprocesses (1) or not (0).
1014 Subprocess execution is denied by default.
1015 Bit 27 of step flags indicates whether the step is allowed to modify its own executable (1) or not (0).
1016 Self-modification is denied by default.
1017 Bits 26-0 are reserved and must be zero.
1018 The dataset table contains D records of 48 bytes each.
1019 Bytes 0-3 contain the dataset index.
1020 Bytes 4-7 contain the dataset name offset in the string table.
1021 Bytes 8-11 contain the dataset name length.
1022 Bytes 12-15 contain the dataset path offset in the string table.
1023 Bytes 16-19 contain the dataset path length.
1024 Bytes 20-23 contain the dataset type.
1025 Dataset type 0x01 is DS_INPUT (read-only source).
1026 Dataset type 0x02 is DS_OUTPUT (write-only result).
1027 Dataset type 0x03 is DS_TEMP (read-write temporary).
1028 Dataset type 0x04 is DS_WORK (read-write workspace).
1029 Dataset type 0x05 is DS_AUDIT (append-only audit log).
1030 Dataset type 0x06 is DS_CONFIG (read-only configuration).
1031 Dataset type 0x07 is DS_LOG (append-only diagnostic log).
1032 Dataset type 0x08 is DS_MANIFEST (read-only manifest reference).
1033 Bytes 24-27 contain the dataset size in bytes (0 for unknown or variable).
1034 Bytes 28-31 contain the dataset block size (default 4096).
1035 Bytes 32-35 contain the dataset record length (0 for unstructured).
1036 Bytes 36-39 contain the dataset record format.
1037 Record format 0x01 is REC_FIXED (fixed-length records).
1038 Record format 0x02 is REC_VARIABLE (variable-length records).
1039 Record format 0x03 is REC_UNDEFINED (unstructured byte stream).
1040 Record format 0x04 is REC_BLOCKED (blocked fixed records).
1041 Bytes 40-43 contain the dataset organization.
1042 Organization 0x01 is ORG_SEQUENTIAL.
1043 Organization 0x02 is ORG_INDEXED.
1044 Organization 0x03 is ORG_RELATIVE.
1045 Organization 0x04 is ORG_DIRECT.
1046 Bytes 44-47 contain dataset flags.
1047 Bit 31 indicates whether the dataset is encrypted (1) or plaintext (0).
1048 Bit 30 indicates whether the dataset is compressed (1) or raw (0).
1049 Bit 29 indicates whether the dataset is append-only (1) or overwrite-allowed (0).
1050 Bit 28 indicates whether the dataset is shared (1) or exclusive (0).
1051 Bits 27-0 are reserved and must be zero.
1052 The environment table contains E records of 32 bytes each.
1053 Bytes 0-3 contain the variable index.
1054 Bytes 4-7 contain the variable name offset in the string table.
1055 Bytes 8-11 contain the variable name length.
1056 Bytes 12-15 contain the variable value offset in the string table.
1057 Bytes 16-19 contain the variable value length.
1058 Bytes 20-23 contain variable flags.
1059 Bit 31 indicates whether the variable is exported (1) or local (0).
1060 Bit 30 indicates whether the variable is read-only (1) or mutable (0).
1061 Bit 29 indicates whether the variable contains a secret (1) or not (0).
1062 Secret variables are masked in audit logs.
1063 Secret variables are not written to diagnostic logs.
1064 Secret variables are not passed to subprocesses.
1065 Bits 28-0 are reserved and must be zero.
1066 The resource limit table contains R records of 16 bytes each.
1067 Bytes 0-3 contain the resource type.
1068 Resource type 0x01 is RES_CPU_TIME.
1069 Resource type 0x02 is RES_MEMORY.
1070 Resource type 0x03 is RES_FILE_SIZE.
1071 Resource type 0x04 is RES_STACK_SIZE.
1072 Resource type 0x05 is RES_DATA_SIZE.
1073 Resource type 0x06 is RES_CORE_SIZE.
1074 Resource type 0x07 is RES_NPROC.
1075 Resource type 0x08 is RES_NOFILE.
1076 Resource type 0x09 is RES_MSGQUEUE.
1077 Resource type 0x0A is RES_NICE.
1078 Resource type 0x0B is RES_RTPRIO.
1079 Resource type 0x0C is RES_RTTIME.
1080 Resource type 0x0D is RES_SIGPENDING.
1081 Resource type 0x0E is RES_MEMLOCK.
1082 Resource type 0x0F is RES_AS.
1083 Resource type 0x10 is RES_LOCKS.
1084 Bytes 4-7 contain the soft limit.
1085 Bytes 8-11 contain the hard limit.
1086 Bytes 12-15 contain limit flags.
1087 Bit 31 indicates whether the limit is enforced (1) or advisory (0).
1088 An enforced limit triggers a termination signal when exceeded.
1089 An advisory limit triggers a warning log when exceeded.
1090 Bits 30-0 are reserved and must be zero.
1091 The security policy table contains P records of 32 bytes each.
1092 Bytes 0-3 contain the policy index.
1093 Bytes 4-7 contain the policy type.
1094 Policy type 0x01 is POLICY_NO_EXEC (prohibit execution of arbitrary commands).
1095 Policy type 0x02 is POLICY_NO_NETWORK (prohibit network access).
1096 Policy type 0x03 is POLICY_NO_FS_ESCAPE (prohibit file system access outside vault).
1097 Policy type 0x04 is POLICY_NO_CREDENTIALS (prohibit credential inference or emission).
1098 Policy type 0x05 is POLICY_NO_SUBPROCESS (prohibit subprocess creation).
1099 Policy type 0x06 is POLICY_NO_SELF_MODIFY (prohibit self-modification).
1100 Policy type 0x07 is POLICY_NO_SETUID (prohibit setuid/setgid operations).
1101 Policy type 0x08 is POLICY_NO_PTRACE (prohibit ptrace and debugging).
1102 Policy type 0x09 is POLICY_NO_MOUNT (prohibit mount/unmount operations).
1103 Policy type 0x0A is POLICY_NO_CHROOT (prohibit chroot operations).
1104 Policy type 0x0B is POLICY_NO_SIGNAL (prohibit signal sending to external processes).
1105 Policy type 0x0C is POLICY_NO_SHARED_MEM (prohibit shared memory outside vault).
1106 Policy type 0x0D is POLICY_NO_MSG_QUEUE (prohibit message queues outside vault).
1107 Policy type 0x0E is POLICY_NO_SEMAPHORE (prohibit semaphores outside vault).
1108 Policy type 0x0F is POLICY_AUDIT_ALL (require audit logging for all operations).
1109 Policy type 0x10 is POLICY_FAIL_CLOSED (require fail-closed behavior on violations).
1110 Bytes 8-11 contain the policy enforcement level.
1111 Enforcement level 0x01 is ENFORCE_DENY (deny the operation).
1112 Enforcement level 0x02 is ENFORCE_LOG (log and allow).
1113 Enforcement level 0x03 is ENFORCE_ABORT (abort the job).
1114 Enforcement level 0x04 is ENFORCE_KILL (kill the process).
1115 Bytes 12-15 contain the policy violation count (0 at start, incremented at runtime).
1116 Bytes 16-19 contain the policy violation limit (0 for unlimited).
1117 Bytes 20-23 contain the policy flags.
1118 Bit 31 indicates whether the policy is active (1) or inactive (0).
1119 Bit 30 indicates whether the policy applies to all steps (1) or selected steps (0).
1120 Bits 29-0 are reserved and must be zero.
1121 Bytes 24-27 contain the number of affected step indices.
1122 Bytes 28-31 contain the affected step list offset.
1123 The affected step list is an array of 32-bit step indices.
1124 The JCL manifest string table follows the same rules as the PTM-IR string table.
1125 The JCL manifest string table contains step names, program names, dataset names, paths, and environment values.
1126 The JCL manifest is validated before job execution.
1127 JCL validation checks magic, version, header checksum, and payload length.
1128 JCL validation checks that all offsets point within the payload bounds.
1129 JCL validation checks that step indices are within [0, J-1].
1130 JCL validation checks that dataset indices are within [0, D-1].
1131 JCL validation checks that dataset paths are absolute and within the vault root.
1132 A dataset path outside the vault root triggers a validation failure.
1133 JCL validation checks that the vault root path is absolute and exists.
1134 JCL validation checks that resource limits are non-negative and hard >= soft.
1135 JCL validation checks that security policies have valid types and enforcement levels.
1136 JCL validation checks that the execution user and group IDs are valid.
1137 JCL validation checks that the umask is a valid 9-bit permission mask.
1138 The umask is validated as (umask & ~0x1FF) == 0.
1139 JCL validation checks that no two datasets have the same path.
1140 Duplicate dataset paths trigger a validation failure.
1141 JCL validation checks that input datasets exist and are readable.
1142 JCL validation checks that output datasets are writable or creatable within the vault.
1143 JCL validation checks that temporary datasets have valid names.
1144 JCL validation checks that the job step dependency graph is acyclic.
1145 A cyclic dependency triggers a validation failure.
1146 JCL validation checks that parallel steps do not write to the same dataset.
1147 Conflicting parallel write access triggers a validation failure.
1148 JCL validation checks that the total memory limit does not exceed system capacity.
1149 JCL validation checks that the total CPU time limit is positive.
1150 JCL validation checks that the wall clock time limit is positive.
1151 JCL validation produces a validation report with magic 0x56414C52 ('VALR').
1152 The JCL validation report follows the same format as the PTM validation report.
1153 The JCL manifest hash is SHA-256 over the complete manifest file.
1154 The JCL manifest hash is recorded in the audit manifest.
1155 The JCL manifest does not contain credentials.
1156 The JCL manifest does not contain secret values.
1157 Secret values are injected at runtime through a secure channel outside the manifest.
1158 The JCL manifest does not reference files outside the vault boundary.
1159 The JCL manifest does not reference network resources.
1160 The JCL manifest does not execute arbitrary commands.
1161 The JCL manifest is read-only after validation.
1162 The JCL manifest is the sole authority for job execution parameters.
1163 Any deviation from the manifest during execution triggers a policy violation.
1164 Policy violations are recorded in the audit trail.
1165 The JCL execution environment enforces the manifest constraints.
1166 The execution environment is a sandbox with namespace isolation.
1167 The sandbox uses chroot to the vault root directory.
1168 The sandbox uses mount namespaces to prevent external file system access.
1169 The sandbox uses network namespaces to prevent external network access.
1170 The sandbox uses PID namespaces to isolate process trees.
1171 The sandbox uses IPC namespaces to isolate inter-process communication.
1172 The sandbox uses user namespaces to map the execution UID/GID.
1173 The sandbox uses seccomp-bpf to filter system calls.
1174 The seccomp filter allows only a whitelist of system calls.
1175 The whitelist includes: read, write, open, close, lseek, stat, fstat, mmap, munmap, brk, exit, exit_group.
1176 The whitelist includes: getpid, getppid, getuid, getgid, geteuid, getegid, nanosleep, clock_gettime.
1177 The whitelist includes: fcntl, dup, dup2, pipe, pipe2, ioctl (limited subset).
1178 The whitelist does not include: socket, connect, bind, listen, accept, send, recv.
1179 The whitelist does not include: execve, execveat, fork, vfork, clone (with shared namespaces).
1180 The whitelist does not include: setuid, setgid, setreuid, setregid, setgroups, capset.
1181 The whitelist does not include: mount, umount, pivot_root, chroot.
1182 The whitelist does not include: ptrace, process_vm_readv, process_vm_writev.
1183 The whitelist does not include: kill, tkill, tgkill (except self-targeted signals).
1184 The whitelist does not include: mknod, mknodat, sysctl, sysfs.
1185 The seccomp filter is loaded before any user code executes.
1186 The seccomp filter is validated for correctness before loading.
