1187 The seccomp filter is part of the vault configuration and is read-only.
1188 The seccomp filter hash is recorded in the audit manifest.
1189 The sandbox boundary is established before the first job step begins.
1190 The sandbox boundary is verified at the start of each job step.
1191 Sandbox boundary verification checks that the current working directory is within the vault.
1192 Sandbox boundary verification checks that open file descriptors point to vault-internal files.
1193 Sandbox boundary verification checks that the process tree contains only vault-internal processes.
1194 Sandbox boundary verification checks that memory mappings are within vault-internal files.
1195 Any sandbox boundary violation triggers POLICY_FAIL_CLOSED enforcement.
1196 The fail-closed action is to terminate the job and record the violation in the audit trail.
1197 The JCL Vault Manifest is the authoritative specification of the sandbox configuration.
1198 The manifest is deterministic: identical job definitions produce identical manifest bytes.
1199 The manifest generation does not use randomness.
1200 The manifest generation records its version in the audit manifest.

SECTION 0007: AUDIT TRAIL BINARY FORMAT
================================================================================

1201 The audit trail records every transformation with provenance.
1202 The audit trail file uses magic 0x41554454 ('AUDT').
1203 The audit trail header is 16 bytes standard plus 64 bytes of audit metadata.
1204 Bytes 16-19 contain the audit record count (A).
1205 Bytes 20-23 contain the number of source artifacts referenced.
1206 Bytes 24-27 contain the number of generated artifacts referenced.
1207 Bytes 28-31 contain the number of validation events.
1208 Bytes 32-35 contain the number of policy violations.
1209 Bytes 36-39 contain the number of complexity claims.
1210 Bytes 40-43 contain the source artifact table offset.
1211 Bytes 44-47 contain the generated artifact table offset.
1212 Bytes 48-51 contain the validation event table offset.
1213 Bytes 52-55 contain the policy violation table offset.
1214 Bytes 56-59 contain the complexity claim table offset.
1215 Bytes 60-63 contain the pipeline execution identifier (64-bit unique ID, high 32 bits).
1216 Bytes 64-67 contain the pipeline execution identifier (low 32 bits).
1217 Bytes 68-71 contain the pipeline start timestamp (64-bit Unix epoch seconds).
1218 Bytes 72-75 contain the pipeline start timestamp nanoseconds (32-bit).
1219 Bytes 76-79 contain the pipeline end timestamp (64-bit Unix epoch seconds, 0 if incomplete).
1220 Bytes 80-83 contain the pipeline end timestamp nanoseconds (32-bit, 0 if incomplete).
1221 Bytes 84-87 contain the pipeline status.
1222 Pipeline status 0x01 is STATUS_RUNNING.
1223 Pipeline status 0x02 is STATUS_SUCCESS.
1224 Pipeline status 0x03 is STATUS_FAILURE.
1225 Pipeline status 0x04 is STATUS_ABORTED.
1226 Pipeline status 0x05 is STATUS_TIMEOUT.
1227 Pipeline status 0x06 is STATUS_POLICY_VIOLATION.
1228 Pipeline status 0x07 is STATUS_VALIDATION_ERROR.
1229 Pipeline status 0x08 is STATUS_SANDBOX_ESCAPE.
1230 Bytes 88-91 contain the agent version (major.minor.patch encoded as 32-bit integer).
1231 Bytes 92-95 contain the host identifier hash (first 4 bytes of SHA-256 of hostname).
1232 Bytes 96-99 contain the vault path offset in the string table.
1233 Bytes 100-103 contain the vault path length.
1234 Bytes 104-107 contain the number of environment variables recorded.
1235 Bytes 108-111 contain the environment variable table offset.
1236 Bytes 112-115 contain the number of configuration hashes recorded.
1237 Bytes 116-119 contain the configuration hash table offset.
1238 Bytes 120-123 contain the number of step execution records.
1239 Bytes 124-127 contain the step execution table offset.
1240 The source artifact table contains one record per input file.
1241 Each source artifact record is 64 bytes.
1242 Bytes 0-3 contain the artifact index.
1243 Bytes 4-7 contain the artifact type.
1244 Artifact type 0x01 is ART_SOURCE_CODE (Fortran source file).
1245 Artifact type 0x02 is ART_TOKEN_STREAM (lexical analyzer output).
1246 Artifact type 0x03 is ART_PTM_IR (intermediate representation).
1247 Artifact type 0x04 is ART_CFG (control flow graph).
1248 Artifact type 0x05 is ART_PTM_TRANSITION (Turing machine table).
1249 Artifact type 0x06 is ART_COMPLEXITY_REPORT (complexity analysis).
1250 Artifact type 0x07 is ART_QUIPPER (quantum circuit).
1251 Artifact type 0x08 is ART_JCL_MANIFEST (execution manifest).
1252 Artifact type 0x09 is ART_AUDIT_TRAIL (this file, self-reference).
1253 Artifact type 0x0A is ART_TEST_VECTOR (test vectors).
1254 Artifact type 0x0B is ART_CONFORMANCE_REPORT (final conformance report).
1255 Artifact type 0x0C is ART_DIAGNOSTIC_LOG (diagnostic output).
1256 Artifact type 0x0D is ART_VALIDATION_REPORT (validation results).
1257 Artifact type 0x0E is ART_SECCOMP_FILTER (sandbox filter).
1258 Artifact type 0x0F is ART_VAULT_CONFIG (vault configuration).
1259 Artifact type 0x10 is ART_ERROR_CODE_TABLE (error definitions).
1260 Artifact type 0x11 is ART_KEYWORD_TABLE (lexical keyword hash table).
1261 Artifact type 0x12 is ART_EBCDIC_TABLE (transcoding table).
1262 Artifact type 0x13 is ART_SNAPSHOT (PTM execution snapshot).
1263 Artifact type 0x14 is ART_OPENQASM (optional OpenQASM translation).
1264 Bytes 8-11 contain the artifact name offset in the string table.
1265 Bytes 12-15 contain the artifact name length.
1266 Bytes 16-19 contain the artifact path offset in the string table.
1267 Bytes 20-23 contain the artifact path length.
1268 Bytes 24-55 contain the SHA-256 hash of the artifact (32 bytes).
1269 Bytes 56-59 contain the artifact size in bytes.
1270 Bytes 60-63 contain artifact flags.
1271 Bit 31 indicates whether the artifact is required (1) or optional (0).
1272 Bit 30 indicates whether the artifact is encrypted (1) or plaintext (0).
1273 Bit 29 indicates whether the artifact is compressed (1) or raw (0).
1274 Bit 28 indicates whether the artifact is deterministic (1) or non-deterministic (0).
1275 Bits 27-0 are reserved and must be zero.
1276 The generated artifact table has identical structure to the source artifact table.
1277 The validation event table contains one record per validation check.
1278 Each validation event record is 48 bytes.
1279 Bytes 0-3 contain the event index.
1280 Bytes 4-7 contain the event timestamp (64-bit Unix epoch seconds, high 32 bits).
1281 Bytes 8-11 contain the event timestamp (low 32 bits).
1282 Bytes 12-15 contain the event timestamp nanoseconds (32-bit).
1283 Bytes 16-19 contain the validation phase.
1284 Validation phase 0x01 is PHASE_LEXICAL.
1285 Validation phase 0x02 is PHASE_SYNTACTIC.
1286 Validation phase 0x03 is PHASE_SEMANTIC.
1287 Validation phase 0x04 is PHASE_TYPE_SYSTEM.
1288 Validation phase 0x05 is PHASE_CONTROL_FLOW.
1289 Validation phase 0x06 is PHASE_PTM_LOWER.
1290 Validation phase 0x07 is PHASE_COMPLEXITY_ANALYSIS.
1291 Validation phase 0x08 is PHASE_QUIPPER_GEN.
1292 Validation phase 0x09 is PHASE_JCL_VALIDATION.
1293 Validation phase 0x0A is PHASE_SANDBOX_VERIFY.
1294 Validation phase 0x0B is PHASE_EXECUTION.
1295 Validation phase 0x0C is PHASE_TESTING.
1296 Validation phase 0x0D is PHASE_AUDIT.
1297 Bytes 20-23 contain the check identifier.
1298 Bytes 24-27 contain the check status (0=PASS, 1=FAIL, 2=WARN, 3=SKIP).
1299 Bytes 28-31 contain the check severity.
1300 Severity 0x01 is SEV_INFO.
1301 Severity 0x02 is SEV_WARNING.
1302 Severity 0x03 is SEV_ERROR.
1303 Severity 0x04 is SEV_FATAL.
1304 Bytes 32-35 contain the error code (0 if no error).
1305 Bytes 36-39 contain the message offset in the string table.
1306 Bytes 40-43 contain the message length.
1307 Bytes 44-47 contain the artifact index referenced by this event (0xFFFFFFFF if none).
1308 The policy violation table contains one record per violation.
1309 Each policy violation record is 48 bytes.
1310 Bytes 0-3 contain the violation index.
1311 Bytes 4-7 contain the violation timestamp (64-bit Unix epoch seconds, high 32 bits).
1312 Bytes 8-11 contain the violation timestamp (low 32 bits).
1313 Bytes 12-15 contain the violation timestamp nanoseconds.
1314 Bytes 16-19 contain the policy type that was violated.
1315 Bytes 20-23 contain the enforcement action taken.
1316 Enforcement action 0x01 is ACTION_DENIED.
1317 Enforcement action 0x02 is ACTION_LOGGED.
1318 Enforcement action 0x03 is ACTION_ABORTED.
1319 Enforcement action 0x04 is ACTION_KILLED.
1320 Bytes 24-27 contain the step index during which the violation occurred.
1321 Bytes 28-31 contain the process ID that caused the violation.
1322 Bytes 32-35 contain the system call number that caused the violation (if applicable).
1323 Bytes 36-39 contain the system call argument that caused the violation (if applicable).
1324 Bytes 40-43 contain the violation description offset in the string table.
1325 Bytes 44-47 contain the violation description length.
1326 The complexity claim table contains one record per complexity assertion.
1327 Each complexity claim record is 64 bytes.
1328 Bytes 0-3 contain the claim index.
1329 Bytes 4-7 contain the claim phase.
1330 Claim phase 0x01 is CLAIM_PTM_TIME.
1331 Claim phase 0x02 is CLAIM_PTM_SPACE.
1332 Claim phase 0x03 is CLAIM_CIRCUIT_SIZE.
1333 Claim phase 0x04 is CLAIM_CIRCUIT_DEPTH.
1334 Claim phase 0x05 is CLAIM_CIRCUIT_VOLUME.
1335 Claim phase 0x06 is CLAIM_LOWER_BOUND.
1336 Claim phase 0x07 is CLAIM_UPPER_BOUND.
1337 Bytes 8-11 contain the polynomial degree d (32-bit unsigned).
1338 Bytes 12-19 contain the constant c (IEEE 754 binary64).
1339 Bytes 20-23 contain the threshold n0 (32-bit unsigned).
1340 Bytes 24-27 contain the claim status.
1341 Claim status 0x01 is CLAIM_PROVEN.
1342 Claim status 0x02 is CLAIM_UNPROVEN.
1343 Claim status 0x03 is CLAIM_DISPROVEN.
1344 Claim status 0x04 is CLAIM_ASSUMED.
1345 Bytes 28-31 contain the input encoding type.
1346 Encoding type 0x01 is ENC_BINARY.
1347 Encoding type 0x02 is ENC_UNARY.
1348 Encoding type 0x03 is ENC_ASCII.
1349 Encoding type 0x04 is ENC_PTM_IR.
1350 Bytes 32-63 contain the claim justification text (32 ASCII characters, space-padded).
1351 The step execution table contains one record per job step.
1352 Each step execution record is 64 bytes.
1353 Bytes 0-3 contain the step index.
1354 Bytes 4-7 contain the step start timestamp (64-bit Unix epoch seconds, high 32 bits).
1355 Bytes 8-11 contain the step start timestamp (low 32 bits).
1356 Bytes 12-15 contain the step start timestamp nanoseconds.
1357 Bytes 16-19 contain the step end timestamp (64-bit Unix epoch seconds, high 32 bits).
1358 Bytes 20-23 contain the step end timestamp (low 32 bits).
1359 Bytes 24-27 contain the step end timestamp nanoseconds.
1360 Bytes 28-31 contain the step exit code.
1361 Bytes 32-35 contain the step CPU time used in milliseconds.
1362 Bytes 36-39 contain the step memory used in bytes (peak resident set).
1363 Bytes 40-43 contain the step disk read bytes.
1364 Bytes 44-47 contain the step disk write bytes.
1365 Bytes 48-51 contain the step network read bytes (always zero in sandbox).
1366 Bytes 52-55 contain the step network write bytes (always zero in sandbox).
1367 Bytes 56-59 contain the step signal count (signals delivered to the step).
1368 Bytes 60-63 contain the step page fault count.
1369 The configuration hash table contains one record per configuration asset.
1370 Each configuration hash record is 48 bytes.
1371 Bytes 0-3 contain the configuration index.
1372 Bytes 4-7 contain the configuration type.
1373 Configuration type 0x01 is CONFIG_SECCOMP_FILTER.
1374 Configuration type 0x02 is CONFIG_KEYWORD_TABLE.
1375 Configuration type 0x03 is CONFIG_EBCDIC_TABLE.
1376 Configuration type 0x04 is CONFIG_ERROR_CODE_TABLE.
1377 Configuration type 0x05 is CONFIG_VAULT_POLICY.
1378 Configuration type 0x06 is CONFIG_AGENT_PARAMETERS.
1379 Bytes 8-39 contain the SHA-256 hash of the configuration file (32 bytes).
1380 Bytes 40-43 contain the configuration file size in bytes.
1381 Bytes 44-47 contain the configuration version.
1382 The audit trail string table follows the same rules as previous string tables.
1383 The audit trail string table contains artifact names, paths, messages, descriptions, and justifications.
1384 The audit trail is append-only during pipeline execution.
1385 The audit trail is flushed to disk after each phase completion.
1386 The audit trail is validated for structural integrity after each append.
1387 Audit trail validation checks magic, version, header checksum, and payload length.
1388 Audit trail validation checks that record counts match the table offsets.
1389 Audit trail validation checks that all string table offsets are within bounds.
1390 Audit trail validation checks that all artifact hashes are present and non-zero.
1391 Audit trail validation checks that timestamps are monotonic within each phase.
1392 Non-monotonic timestamps trigger a validation warning.
1393 The audit trail does not fabricate timestamps.
1394 Timestamps are obtained from the execution environment's clock source.
1395 The clock source is recorded in the audit metadata.
1396 The audit trail does not contain credentials.
1397 The audit trail does not contain secret values.
1398 Secret values in environment variables are masked with asterisks.
1399 The audit trail hash is SHA-256 over the complete audit trail file.
1400 The audit trail hash is recorded in the conformance report.

SECTION 0008: VALIDATION PROTOCOL BINARY SPECIFICATION
================================================================================

1401 The validation protocol defines the binary format and procedures for all validation checks.
1402 The validation protocol file uses magic 0x56414C50 ('VALP').
1403 The validation protocol header is 16 bytes standard plus 32 bytes of protocol metadata.
1404 Bytes 16-19 contain the protocol version (0x00000001).
1405 Bytes 20-23 contain the number of validation suites (S).
1406 Bytes 24-27 contain the number of validation rules (R).
1407 Bytes 28-31 contain the number of validation checkpoints (C).
1408 Bytes 32-35 contain the validation suite table offset.
1409 Bytes 36-39 contain the validation rule table offset.
1410 Bytes 40-43 contain the validation checkpoint table offset.
1411 Bytes 44-47 contain the number of artifact validators.
1412 Bytes 48-51 contain the artifact validator table offset.
1413 The validation suite table contains S records of 32 bytes each.
1414 Bytes 0-3 contain the suite index.
1415 Bytes 4-7 contain the suite name offset in the string table.
1416 Bytes 8-11 contain the suite name length.
1417 Bytes 12-15 contain the suite phase.
1418 Suite phase values match the validation phase enumeration from the audit trail.
1419 Bytes 16-19 contain the number of rules in this suite.
1420 Bytes 20-23 contain the rule list offset.
1421 Bytes 24-27 contain the suite priority (lower number = higher priority).
1422 Bytes 28-31 contain suite flags.
1423 Bit 31 indicates whether the suite is fail-closed (1) or fail-open (0).
1424 A fail-closed suite aborts the pipeline on the first rule failure.
1425 A fail-open suite continues execution and logs all failures.
1426 Bit 30 indicates whether the suite is mandatory (1) or optional (0).
1427 A mandatory suite must execute; skipping it triggers a validation failure.
1428 Bits 29-0 are reserved and must be zero.
1429 The validation rule table contains R records of 48 bytes each.
1430 Bytes 0-3 contain the rule index.
1431 Bytes 4-7 contain the rule name offset in the string table.
1432 Bytes 8-11 contain the rule name length.
1433 Bytes 12-15 contain the parent suite index.
1434 Bytes 16-19 contain the rule type.
1435 Rule type 0x01 is RULE_STRUCTURE (check file structure and headers).
1436 Rule type 0x02 is RULE_BOUNDS (check array and pointer bounds).
1437 Rule type 0x03 is RULE_TYPE (check type consistency and compatibility).
1438 Rule type 0x04 is RULE_CONTROL_FLOW (check CFG properties).
1439 Rule type 0x05 is RULE_DETERMINISM (check output determinism).
1440 Rule type 0x06 is RULE_HASH (check artifact hash integrity).
1441 Rule type 0x07 is RULE_MAGIC (check magic numbers).
1442 Rule type 0x08 is RULE_VERSION (check version compatibility).
1443 Rule type 0x09 is RULE_CHECKSUM (check CRC-32 and other checksums).
1444 Rule type 0x0A is RULE_SEMANTIC (check semantic equivalence).
1445 Rule type 0x0B is RULE_COMPLEXITY (check complexity claims).
1446 Rule type 0x0C is RULE_SANDBOX (check sandbox boundary).
1447 Rule type 0x0D is RULE_CREDENTIAL (check for credential leakage).
1448 Rule type 0x0E is RULE_REACHABILITY (check state reachability).
1449 Rule type 0x0F is RULE_ORACLE (check oracle assumptions).
1450 Rule type 0x10 is RULE_UNITARY (check quantum unitarity).
1451 Rule type 0x11 is RULE_ACYCLIC (check for cycles in graphs).
1452 Rule type 0x12 is RULE_MONOTONIC (check timestamp monotonicity).
1453 Rule type 0x13 is RULE_UNIQUE (check uniqueness constraints).
1454 Rule type 0x14 is RULE_ALIGNMENT (check memory alignment).
1455 Rule type 0x15 is RULE_ENCODING (check character encoding validity).
1456 Rule type 0x16 is RULE_RESERVED_BITS (check reserved bits are zero).
1457 Rule type 0x17 is RULE_DEPENDENCY (check dependency graph acyclicity).
1458 Rule type 0x18 is RULE_PARALLEL_SAFE (check parallel step safety).
1459 Rule type 0x19 is RULE_PATH (check file path validity and vault containment).
1460 Rule type 0x1A is RULE_SYSCALL (check system call whitelist compliance).
1461 Rule type 0x1B is RULE_RESOURCE (check resource limit compliance).
1462 Rule type 0x1C is RULE_SCOPE (check symbol scope nesting limits).
1463 Rule type 0x1D is RULE_LOOP (check loop nesting depth limits).
1464 Rule type 0x1E is RULE_DEAD_CODE (check for unreachable code).
1465 Rule type 0x1F is RULE_DANGLING (check for dangling wires or references).
1466 Bytes 20-23 contain the rule severity.
1467 Severity values match the severity enumeration from the audit trail.
1468 Bytes 24-27 contain the rule check function offset.
1469 The check function offset points to a bytecode sequence in the validator bytecode section.
1470 Bytes 28-31 contain the rule check function length in bytes.
1471 Bytes 32-35 contain the expected result (0x00 for PASS, 0x01 for FAIL, 0x02 for WARN).
1472 Bytes 36-39 contain the rule retry count (0 for no retries).
1473 Bytes 40-43 contain the rule timeout in milliseconds (0 for no timeout).
1474 Bytes 44-47 contain rule flags.
1475 Bit 31 indicates whether the rule is enabled (1) or disabled (0).
1476 Bit 30 indicates whether the rule result is cached (1) or recomputed (0).
1477 Bit 29 indicates whether the rule requires network access (1) or not (0).
1478 Bit 28 indicates whether the rule requires external data (1) or not (0).
1479 Bits 27-0 are reserved and must be zero.
1480 The validation checkpoint table contains C records of 16 bytes each.
1481 Bytes 0-3 contain the checkpoint index.
1482 Bytes 4-7 contain the checkpoint phase.
1483 Bytes 8-11 contain the checkpoint rule count.
1484 Bytes 12-15 contain the checkpoint artifact index (0xFFFFFFFF if global).
1485 Checkpoints are inserted between pipeline phases.
1486 A checkpoint validates all rules in its phase before allowing the next phase to begin.
1487 The artifact validator table contains one record per validator.
1488 Each artifact validator record is 32 bytes.
1489 Bytes 0-3 contain the validator index.
1490 Bytes 4-7 contain the validator artifact type.
1491 Bytes 8-11 contain the validator magic number expected.
1492 Bytes 12-15 contain the validator minimum version.
1493 Bytes 16-19 contain the validator maximum version.
1494 Bytes 20-23 contain the validator header size.
1495 Bytes 24-27 contain the validator payload alignment (must be power of two).
1496 Bytes 28-31 contain validator flags.
1497 Bit 31 indicates whether the validator requires checksum verification (1) or not (0).
1498 Bit 30 indicates whether the validator requires hash verification (1) or not (0).
1499 Bit 29 indicates whether the validator requires deterministic output (1) or not (0).
1500 Bits 28-0 are reserved and must be zero.
1501 The validator bytecode section contains executable validation logic.
1502 The bytecode is a stack-based virtual machine with 16 opcodes.
1503 Opcode 0x00 is OP_NOP (no operation).
1504 Opcode 0x01 is OP_LOAD (load 32-bit value from artifact at offset).
1505 Opcode 0x02 is OP_LOAD64 (load 64-bit value from artifact at offset).
1506 Opcode 0x03 is OP_LOADF (load IEEE 754 binary64 from artifact at offset).
1507 Opcode 0x04 is OP_PUSH (push immediate 32-bit value onto stack).
1508 Opcode 0x05 is OP_CMP (compare top two stack values, push result).
1509 Opcode 0x06 is OP_JZ (jump if zero to relative offset).
1510 Opcode 0x07 is OP_JNZ (jump if non-zero to relative offset).
1511 Opcode 0x08 is OP_JMP (unconditional jump to relative offset).
1512 Opcode 0x09 is OP_AND (bitwise AND of top two stack values).
1513 Opcode 0x0A is OP_OR (bitwise OR of top two stack values).
1514 Opcode 0x0B is OP_XOR (bitwise XOR of top two stack values).
1515 Opcode 0x0C is OP_NOT (bitwise NOT of top stack value).
1516 Opcode 0x0D is OP_ADD (arithmetic add of top two stack values).
1517 Opcode 0x0E is OP_SUB (arithmetic subtract of top two stack values).
1518 Opcode 0x0F is OP_HASH (compute SHA-256 of artifact region and push digest).
1519 Each opcode is encoded as one byte followed by zero or more operand bytes.
1520 OP_LOAD takes 4 bytes: 1 byte opcode, 4 bytes offset.
1521 OP_LOAD64 takes 4 bytes: 1 byte opcode, 4 bytes offset.
1522 OP_LOADF takes 4 bytes: 1 byte opcode, 4 bytes offset.
1523 OP_PUSH takes 5 bytes: 1 byte opcode, 4 bytes immediate value.
1524 OP_CMP takes 1 byte: 1 byte opcode, no operands.
1525 OP_JZ takes 5 bytes: 1 byte opcode, 4 bytes signed relative offset.
1526 OP_JNZ takes 5 bytes: 1 byte opcode, 4 bytes signed relative offset.
1527 OP_JMP takes 5 bytes: 1 byte opcode, 4 bytes signed relative offset.
1528 OP_AND, OP_OR, OP_XOR, OP_NOT, OP_ADD, OP_SUB each take 1 byte.
1529 OP_HASH takes 9 bytes: 1 byte opcode, 4 bytes offset, 4 bytes length.
1530 The stack is a 32-bit unsigned integer array with maximum depth 256.
1531 Stack overflow triggers a validation failure.
1532 Stack underflow triggers a validation failure.
1533 The bytecode execution halts when it reaches the end of the function or an OP_JMP to the end.
1534 The result of the bytecode execution is the top value on the stack.
1535 A result of zero indicates PASS.
1536 A non-zero result indicates FAIL with the value as the error code.
1537 The validation protocol is validated before use.
1538 Protocol validation checks magic, version, header checksum, and payload length.
1539 Protocol validation checks that suite indices are within [0, S-1].
1540 Protocol validation checks that rule parent suite indices are valid.
1541 Protocol validation checks that checkpoint phases are within the defined enumeration.
1542 Protocol validation checks that bytecode offsets and lengths are within the payload bounds.
1543 Protocol validation checks that opcode bytes are within [0x00, 0x0F].
1544 Protocol validation checks that jump offsets do not point outside the function bounds.
1545 Protocol validation checks that artifact validator magic numbers match known values.
1546 Protocol validation checks that artifact validator versions are within [min, max].
1547 Protocol validation checks that artifact validator alignments are powers of two.
1548 The validation protocol file hash is SHA-256 over the complete protocol file.
1549 The validation protocol file hash is recorded in the audit manifest.
1550 The validation protocol is read-only after validation.

SECTION 0009: TESTING BINARY VECTORS
================================================================================

1551 The testing framework verifies correctness through binary test vectors.
1552 The test vector file uses magic 0x54455354 ('TEST').
1553 The test vector header is 16 bytes standard plus 48 bytes of test metadata.
1554 Bytes 16-19 contain the number of test cases (T).
1555 Bytes 20-23 contain the number of test suites (S).
1556 Bytes 24-27 contain the test framework version.
1557 Bytes 28-31 contain the test vector table offset.
1558 Bytes 32-35 contain the expected output table offset.
1559 Bytes 36-39 contain the actual output table offset.
1560 Bytes 40-43 contain the comparison mask table offset.
1561 Bytes 44-47 contain the test result table offset.
1562 Bytes 48-51 contain the number of oracle test vectors.
1563 Bytes 52-55 contain the oracle vector table offset.
1564 Bytes 56-59 contain the number of boundary test vectors.
1565 Bytes 60-63 contain the boundary vector table offset.
1566 The test vector table contains T records of 64 bytes each.
1567 Bytes 0-3 contain the test case index.
1568 Bytes 4-7 contain the test suite index.
1569 Bytes 8-11 contain the test case name offset in the string table.
1570 Bytes 12-15 contain the test case name length.
1571 Bytes 16-19 contain the test case type.
1572 Test case type 0x01 is TEST_CANONICAL (canonical sum-to-100 example).
1573 Test case type 0x02 is TEST_EMPTY_LOOP (loop with zero iterations).
1574 Test case type 0x03 is TEST_SINGLE_ITERATION (loop with exactly one iteration).
1575 Test case type 0x04 is TEST_INVALID_BOUND (negative or invalid loop bound).
1576 Test case type 0x05 is TEST_INTEGER_OVERFLOW (arithmetic overflow).
1577 Test case type 0x06 is TEST_TYPE_MISMATCH (incompatible types).
1578 Test case type 0x07 is TEST_UNREACHABLE_CODE (dead code paths).
1579 Test case type 0x08 is TEST_MALFORMED_INPUT (syntactically invalid source).
1580 Test case type 0x09 is TEST_MISSING_DECLARATION (undeclared variable).
1581 Test case type 0x0A is TEST_INVALID_QUIPPER (malformed quantum circuit).
1582 Test case type 0x0B is TEST_SANDBOX_VIOLATION (attempted boundary escape).
1583 Test case type 0x0C is TEST_CREDENTIAL_LEAK (credential in output).
1584 Test case type 0x0D is TEST_DETERMINISM (identical inputs produce identical outputs).
1585 Test case type 0x0E is TEST_HASH_STABILITY (hash consistency across runs).
1586 Test case type 0x0F is TEST_COMPLEXITY_BOUND (polynomial bound verification).
1587 Test case type 0x10 is TEST_CONTROL_FLOW (CFG correctness).
1588 Test case type 0x11 is TEST_TYPE_SYSTEM (type checking correctness).
1589 Test case type 0x12 is TEST_LOOP_NESTING (deep loop nesting).
1590 Test case type 0x13 is TEST_SCOPE_DEPTH (deep scope nesting).
1591 Test case type 0x14 is TEST_ARRAY_BOUNDS (array index out of bounds).
1592 Test case type 0x15 is TEST_RECURSION (recursive subroutine).
1593 Test case type 0x16 is TEST_PARALLEL_SAFE (parallel execution safety).
1594 Test case type 0x17 is TEST_RESOURCE_LIMIT (resource exhaustion).
1595 Test case type 0x18 is TEST_SIGNAL_HANDLING (signal delivery and handling).
1596 Test case type 0x19 is TEST_MEMORY_ALIGNMENT (unaligned memory access).
1597 Test case type 0x1A is TEST_RESERVED_BITS (non-zero reserved bits).
1598 Test case type 0x1B is TEST_PATH_ESCAPE (file path outside vault).
1599 Test case type 0x1C is TEST_SYSCALL_FILTER (prohibited system call).
1600 Test case type 0x1D is TEST_NETWORK_ACCESS (attempted network operation).
1601 Test case type 0x1E is TEST_SELF_MODIFY (attempted self-modification).
1602 Test case type 0x1F is TEST_SETUID (attempted privilege escalation).
1603 Test case type 0x20 is TEST_PTRACE (attempted process tracing).
1604 Test case type 0x21 is TEST_MOUNT (attempted mount operation).
1605 Test case type 0x22 is TEST_CHROOT (attempted chroot operation).
1606 Test case type 0x23 is TEST_SHARED_MEM (attempted shared memory outside vault).
1607 Test case type 0x24 is TEST_MSG_QUEUE (attempted message queue outside vault).
1608 Test case type 0x25 is TEST_SEMAPHORE (attempted semaphore outside vault).
1609 Test case type 0x26 is TEST_ORACLE_ASSUMPTION (unstated oracle assumption).
1610 Test case type 0x27 is TEST_UNITARY_VIOLATION (non-unitary quantum matrix).
1611 Test case type 0x28 is TEST_CYCLE_DETECTION (cyclic dependency or call graph).
1612 Test case type 0x29 is TEST_TIMESTAMP_MONOTONIC (non-monotonic timestamps).
1613 Test case type 0x2A is TEST_DUPLICATE_SYMBOL (duplicate symbol in same scope).
1614 Test case type 0x2B is TEST_DUPLICATE_DATASET (duplicate dataset path).
1615 Test case type 0x2C is TEST_DEPENDENCY_CYCLE (cyclic job step dependency).
1616 Test case type 0x2D is TEST_PARALLEL_CONFLICT (conflicting parallel dataset access).
1617 Test case type 0x2E is TEST_UMASK_INVALID (invalid umask value).
1618 Test case type 0x2F is TEST_VERSION_MISMATCH (incompatible artifact version).
1619 Test case type 0x30 is TEST_MAGIC_MISMATCH (incorrect magic number).
1620 Test case type 0x31 is TEST_CHECKSUM_FAIL (CRC-32 or hash mismatch).
1621 Test case type 0x32 is TEST_ENCODING_INVALID (invalid character encoding).
1622 Test case type 0x33 is TEST_ALIGNMENT_VIOLATION (memory alignment error).
1623 Test case type 0x34 is TEST_STACK_OVERFLOW (validation stack overflow).
1624 Test case type 0x35 is TEST_STACK_UNDERFLOW (validation stack underflow).
1625 Test case type 0x36 is TEST_BYTECODE_INVALID (invalid validation bytecode).
1626 Test case type 0x37 is TEST_JUMP_OUT_OF_BOUNDS (validation jump out of function).
1627 Test case type 0x38 is TEST_OPCODE_INVALID (unrecognized validation opcode).
1628 Test case type 0x39 is TEST_PAYLOAD_TRUNCATED (artifact payload shorter than header claims).
1629 Test case type 0x3A is TEST_PAYLOAD_EXCESS (artifact payload longer than header claims).
1630 Test case type 0x3B is TEST_HEADER_CORRUPT (header checksum mismatch).
1631 Test case type 0x3C is TEST_RESERVED_BIT_SET (reserved bit set to one).
1632 Test case type 0x3D is TEST_PROHIBITED_HEAD_MOVE (PTM head movement violation).
1633 Test case type 0x3E is TEST_NONDETERMINISTIC_PTM (non-deterministic transition table).
1634 Test case type 0x3F is TEST_UNREACHABLE_STATE (PTM state unreachable from initial).
1635 Test case type 0x40 is TEST_DEAD_STATE (PTM state with no path to halting).
1636 Test case type 0x41 is TEST_COMPLEXITY_UNPROVEN (complexity claim without proof).
1637 Test case type 0x42 is TEST_HARD_CODED_RESULT (result derived from constant instead of computation).
1638 Test case type 0x43 is TEST_OPENQASM_PRIMARY (OpenQASM used as primary output instead of Quipper).
1639 Test case type 0x44 is TEST_LAYER_COLLAPSE (classical and quantum layers collapsed into one).
1640 Test case type 0x45 is TEST_SEMANTIC_CHANGE (transformation altered observable behavior).
1641 Test case type 0x46 is TEST_MISSING_PROVENANCE (artifact lacks provenance record).
1642 Test case type 0x47 is TEST_HASH_MISMATCH (artifact hash does not match recorded hash).
1643 Test case type 0x48 is TEST_TIMESTAMP_FABRICATED (timestamp not from environment clock).
1644 Test case type 0x49 is TEST_CREDENTIAL_IN_AUDIT (credential found in audit trail).
1645 Test case type 0x4A is TEST_SECRET_IN_MANIFEST (secret found in JCL manifest).
1646 Test case type 0x4B is TEST_NETWORK_IN_MANIFEST (network resource in JCL manifest).
1647 Test case type 0x4C is TEST_FS_ESCAPE_IN_MANIFEST (file path outside vault in manifest).
1648 Test case type 0x4D is TEST_ARBITRARY_COMMAND (arbitrary command execution detected).
1649 Test case type 0x4E is TEST_CREDENTIAL_INFERENCE (credential inference attempt detected).
1650 Test case type 0x4F is TEST_SANDBOX_ESCAPE (sandbox boundary violation detected).
1651 Bytes 20-23 of a test vector record contain the input data offset.
1652 Bytes 24-27 contain the input data length in bytes.
1653 Bytes 28-31 contain the expected status.
1654 Expected status 0x01 is EXPECT_PASS (test should succeed).
1655 Expected status 0x02 is EXPECT_FAIL (test should fail with error).
1656 Expected status 0x03 is EXPECT_WARN (test should produce warning but continue).
1657 Expected status 0x04 is EXPECT_ABORT (test should abort the pipeline).
1658 Bytes 32-35 contain the expected error code (0 if no specific error expected).
1659 Bytes 36-39 contain the expected validation phase (0 if not applicable).
1660 Bytes 40-43 contain the test timeout in milliseconds.
1661 Bytes 44-47 contain the test priority (lower = higher priority).
1662 Bytes 48-51 contain the test dependency count.
1663 Bytes 52-55 contain the test dependency list offset.
1664 Bytes 56-59 contain the number of iterations (for stress tests, default 1).
1665 Bytes 60-63 contain test flags.
1666 Bit 31 indicates whether the test is enabled (1) or disabled (0).
1667 Bit 30 indicates whether the test is deterministic (1) or stochastic (0).
1668 Bit 29 indicates whether the test requires network (1) or not (0).
1669 Bit 28 indicates whether the test requires external files (1) or not (0).
1670 Bits 27-0 are reserved and must be zero.
1671 The expected output table contains one record per test case.
1672 Each expected output record is 32 bytes.
1673 Bytes 0-3 contain the test case index.
1674 Bytes 4-7 contain the output artifact type expected.
1675 Bytes 8-11 contain the output hash expected (first 4 bytes of SHA-256).
1676 Bytes 12-15 contain the output size expected in bytes.
1677 Bytes 16-19 contain the output magic expected.
1678 Bytes 20-23 contain the output version expected.
1679 Bytes 24-27 contain the output checksum expected (CRC-32).
1680 Bytes 28-31 contain output flags.
1681 Bit 31 indicates whether exact byte match is required (1) or structural match (0).
1682 Bit 30 indicates whether hash match is required (1) or not (0).
1683 Bit 29 indicates whether size match is required (1) or not (0).
1684 Bits 28-0 are reserved and must be zero.
1685 The actual output table has identical structure to the expected output table.
1686 The comparison mask table contains one 32-byte mask per test case.
1687 Each byte in the mask corresponds to one field in the output record.
1688 A mask byte of 0xFF means the field must match exactly.
1689 A mask byte of 0x00 means the field is ignored.
1690 Intermediate values mean partial matching (not used in this revision).
1691 The test result table contains one record per test case execution.
1692 Each test result record is 48 bytes.
1693 Bytes 0-3 contain the test case index.
1694 Bytes 4-7 contain the execution timestamp (64-bit Unix epoch seconds, high 32 bits).
1695 Bytes 8-11 contain the execution timestamp (low 32 bits).
1696 Bytes 12-15 contain the execution timestamp nanoseconds.
1697 Bytes 16-19 contain the actual status.
1698 Actual status 0x01 is RESULT_PASS.
1699 Actual status 0x02 is RESULT_FAIL.
1700 Actual status 0x03 is RESULT_WARN.
1701 Actual status 0x04 is RESULT_ABORT.
1702 Actual status 0x05 is RESULT_TIMEOUT.
1703 Actual status 0x06 is RESULT_SKIP.
1704 Actual status 0x07 is RESULT_ERROR (test framework internal error).
1705 Bytes 20-23 contain the actual error code.
1706 Bytes 24-27 contain the actual validation phase.
1707 Bytes 28-31 contain the execution time in milliseconds.
1708 Bytes 32-35 contain the memory used in bytes.
1709 Bytes 36-39 contain the CPU time in milliseconds.
1710 Bytes 40-43 contain the disk read bytes.
1711 Bytes 44-47 contain the disk write bytes.
1712 The oracle test vector table contains vectors for quantum oracle testing.
1713 Each oracle vector record is 32 bytes.
1714 Bytes 0-3 contain the oracle vector index.
1715 Bytes 4-7 contain the oracle index referenced.
1716 Bytes 8-11 contain the input qubit state offset.
1717 Bytes 12-15 contain the input qubit state length in bytes.
1718 Bytes 16-19 contain the expected output qubit state offset.
1719 Bytes 20-23 contain the expected output qubit state length.
1720 Bytes 24-27 contain the oracle test type.
1721 Oracle test type 0x01 is ORACLE_IDENTITY (oracle should act as identity).
1722 Oracle test type 0x02 is ORACLE_INVERT (oracle should invert phase).
1723 Oracle test type 0x03 is ORACLE_BALANCED (oracle should be balanced).
1724 Oracle test type 0x04 is ORACLE_CONSTANT (oracle should be constant).
1725 Bytes 28-31 contain oracle vector flags.
1726 Bit 31 indicates whether the oracle is reversible (1) or not (0).
1727 Bits 30-0 are reserved and must be zero.
1728 The boundary test vector table contains vectors for sandbox boundary testing.
1729 Each boundary vector record is 32 bytes.
1730 Bytes 0-3 contain the boundary vector index.
1731 Bytes 4-7 contain the boundary test type.
1732 Boundary test type 0x01 is BOUNDARY_CHDIR (attempt to change directory outside vault).
1733 Boundary test type 0x02 is BOUNDARY_OPEN (attempt to open file outside vault).
1734 Boundary test type 0x03 is BOUNDARY_CREAT (attempt to create file outside vault).
1735 Boundary test type 0x04 is BOUNDARY_UNLINK (attempt to delete file outside vault).
1736 Boundary test type 0x05 is BOUNDARY_RENAME (attempt to rename across vault boundary).
1737 Boundary test type 0x06 is BOUNDARY_SYMLINK (attempt to create symlink outside vault).
1738 Boundary test type 0x07 is BOUNDARY_HARDLINK (attempt to create hardlink outside vault).
1739 Boundary test type 0x08 is BOUNDARY_MMAP (attempt to mmap file outside vault).
1740 Boundary test type 0x09 is BOUNDARY_EXEC (attempt to execute file outside vault).
1741 Boundary test type 0x0A is BOUNDARY_SOCKET (attempt to create socket).
1742 Boundary test type 0x0B is BOUNDARY_CONNECT (attempt to connect to external address).
1743 Boundary test type 0x0C is BOUNDARY_BIND (attempt to bind to external address).
1744 Boundary test type 0x0D is BOUNDARY_SEND (attempt to send data over network).
1745 Boundary test type 0x0E is BOUNDARY_RECV (attempt to receive data over network).
1746 Boundary test type 0x0F is BOUNDARY_FORK (attempt to fork process).
1747 Boundary test type 0x10 is BOUNDARY_CLONE (attempt to clone process with shared ns).
1748 Boundary test type 0x11 is BOUNDARY_PTRACE (attempt to ptrace external process).
1749 Boundary test type 0x12 is BOUNDARY_KILL (attempt to signal external process).
1750 Boundary test type 0x13 is BOUNDARY_SETUID (attempt to change user ID).
1751 Boundary test type 0x14 is BOUNDARY_SETGID (attempt to change group ID).
1752 Boundary test type 0x15 is BOUNDARY_MOUNT (attempt to mount filesystem).
1753 Boundary test type 0x16 is BOUNDARY_UMOUNT (attempt to unmount filesystem).
1754 Boundary test type 0x17 is BOUNDARY_CHROOT (attempt to change root directory).
1755 Boundary test type 0x18 is BOUNDARY_PIVOT_ROOT (attempt to pivot root).
1756 Boundary test type 0x19 is BOUNDARY_MKMOD (attempt to create device node).
1757 Boundary test type 0x1A is BOUNDARY_SYSCTL (attempt to modify kernel parameter).
1758 Boundary test type 0x1B is BOUNDARY_SYSFS (attempt to access sysfs).
1759 Boundary test type 0x1C is BOUNDARY_PROC (attempt to access /proc outside vault).
1760 Boundary test type 0x1D is BOUNDARY_DEV (attempt to access /dev outside vault).
1761 Boundary test type 0x1E is BOUNDARY_TMP (attempt to access /tmp outside vault).
1762 Boundary test type 0x1F is BOUNDARY_HOME (attempt to access user home outside vault).
1763 Boundary test type 0x20 is BOUNDARY_ENV (attempt to read secret environment variable).
1764 Bytes 8-11 contain the boundary test argument offset.
1765 Bytes 12-15 contain the boundary test argument length.
1766 Bytes 16-19 contain the expected enforcement action.
1767 Bytes 20-23 contain the actual enforcement action.
1768 Bytes 24-27 contain the boundary test result.
1769 Boundary test result 0x01 is BOUNDARY_BLOCKED (operation was blocked).
1770 Boundary test result 0x02 is BOUNDARY_ALLOWED (operation was incorrectly allowed).
1771 Boundary test result 0x03 is BOUNDARY_ERROR (test framework error).
1772 Bytes 28-31 contain boundary vector flags.
1773 Bit 31 indicates whether the test is destructive (1) or safe (0).
1774 Destructive tests are run in isolated temporary vaults.
1775 Bits 30-0 are reserved and must be zero.
1776 The test vector file is validated before execution.
1777 Test vector validation checks magic, version, header checksum, and payload length.
1778 Test vector validation checks that test case indices are unique and within range.
1779 Test vector validation checks that test suite indices are valid.
1780 Test vector validation checks that input data offsets are within payload bounds.
1781 Test vector validation checks that expected status values are within the defined enumeration.
1782 Test vector validation checks that test dependencies form a DAG.
1783 Cyclic test dependencies trigger a validation failure.
1784 Test vector validation checks that oracle vectors reference valid oracle indices.
1785 Test vector validation checks that boundary vectors reference valid boundary test types.
1786 The test vector file hash is SHA-256 over the complete test vector file.
1787 The test vector file hash is recorded in the audit manifest.
1788 The canonical test case is test case index 0 with type TEST_CANONICAL.
1789 The canonical test case input is the binary encoding of the canonical Fortran source.
1790 The canonical Fortran source is: PROGRAM SUM; INTEGER I, SUM; SUM = 0; DO I = 1, 100; SUM = SUM + I; END DO; END PROGRAM SUM.
1791 The canonical test case expected output is the PTM-IR file with the computed sum of 5050.
1792 The canonical test case expected status is EXPECT_PASS.
1793 The canonical test case expected validation phase is PHASE_EXECUTION.
1794 The canonical test case expected error code is 0.
1795 The empty loop test case uses DO I = 1, 0 with zero iterations.
1796 The empty loop test case expected sum is 0.
1797 The single iteration test case uses DO I = 1, 1 with one iteration.
1798 The single iteration test case expected sum is 1.
1799 The invalid bound test case uses DO I = 1, -1 with negative bound.
1800 The invalid bound test case expected behavior is zero iterations (Fortran DO semantics).

SECTION 0010: SECURITY BOUNDARY BINARY CHECKS
================================================================================

1801 The security boundary is enforced at the binary level through continuous verification.
1802 The security boundary file uses magic 0x53454342 ('SECB').
1803 The security boundary header is 16 bytes standard plus 64 bytes of security metadata.
1804 Bytes 16-19 contain the boundary version (0x00000001).
1805 Bytes 20-23 contain the number of boundary checks (B).
1806 Bytes 24-27 contain the number of active enforcement policies.
1807 Bytes 28-31 contain the number of sandbox namespaces.
1808 Bytes 32-35 contain the boundary check table offset.
1809 Bytes 36-39 contain the enforcement policy table offset.
1810 Bytes 40-43 contain the namespace table offset.
1811 Bytes 44-47 contain the number of file descriptor checks.
1812 Bytes 48-51 contain the file descriptor check table offset.
1813 Bytes 52-55 contain the number of memory region checks.
1814 Bytes 56-59 contain the memory region check table offset.
1815 Bytes 60-63 contain the number of network endpoint checks.
1816 Bytes 64-67 contain the network endpoint check table offset.
1817 Bytes 68-71 contain the number of capability checks.
1818 Bytes 72-75 contain the capability check table offset.
1819 Bytes 76-79 contain the security audit log offset.
1820 Bytes 80-83 contain the security audit log length.
1821 Bytes 84-87 contain the last verification timestamp (64-bit Unix epoch seconds, high 32 bits).
1822 Bytes 88-91 contain the last verification timestamp (low 32 bits).
1823 Bytes 92-95 contain the last verification timestamp nanoseconds.
1824 Bytes 96-99 contain the verification interval in milliseconds (default 1000).
1825 Bytes 100-103 contain the maximum allowed verification drift in milliseconds (default 100).
1826 Bytes 104-107 contain the number of verification failures tolerated (default 0).
1827 Bytes 108-111 contain the current verification failure count.
1828 Bytes 112-115 contain the security state.
1829 Security state 0x01 is STATE_SECURE (all checks passing).
1830 Security state 0x02 is STATE_DEGRADED (some checks warning).
1831 Security state 0x03 is STATE_BREACHED (check failure detected).
1832 Security state 0x04 is STATE_RECOVERED (breach contained and recovered).
1833 Security state 0x05 is STATE_UNKNOWN (verification not yet performed).
1834 Bytes 116-119 contain the breach containment action.
1835 Containment action 0x01 is CONTAIN_NONE (no action, log only).
1836 Containment action 0x02 is CONTAIN_PAUSE (pause job execution).
1837 Containment action 0x03 is CONTAIN_KILL (terminate all processes).
1838 Containment action 0x04 is CONTAIN_ISOLATE (isolate process in separate namespace).
1839 Containment action 0x05 is CONTAIN_ROLLBACK (rollback to last known good state).
1840 Bytes 120-123 contain the number of whitelisted system calls.
1841 Bytes 124-127 contain the syscall whitelist offset.
1842 The boundary check table contains B records of 32 bytes each.
1843 Bytes 0-3 contain the check index.
1844 Bytes 4-7 contain the check type.
1845 Check type 0x01 is CHECK_CWD (current working directory within vault).
1846 Check type 0x02 is CHECK_OPEN_FD (open file descriptors point to vault files).
1847 Check type 0x03 is CHECK_MMAP (memory mappings are within vault files).
1848 Check type 0x04 is CHECK_PROC_TREE (process tree contains only vault processes).
1849 Check type 0x05 is CHECK_NS_MOUNT (mount namespace is isolated).
1850 Check type 0x06 is CHECK_NS_NET (network namespace is isolated).
1851 Check type 0x07 is CHECK_NS_PID (PID namespace is isolated).
1852 Check type 0x08 is CHECK_NS_IPC (IPC namespace is isolated).
1853 Check type 0x09 is CHECK_NS_USER (user namespace is properly mapped).
1854 Check type 0x0A is CHECK_SECCOMP (seccomp filter is loaded and active).
1855 Check type 0x0B is CHECK_CAP (process capabilities are dropped).
1856 Check type 0x0C is CHECK_UID (process UID matches manifest).
1857 Check type 0x0D is CHECK_GID (process GID matches manifest).
1858 Check type 0x0E is CHECK_RLIMIT (resource limits are enforced).
1859 Check type 0x0F is CHECK_NO_NEW_PRIVS (PR_SET_NO_NEW_PRIVS is set).
1860 Check type 0x10 is CHECK_DUMPABLE (process dumpable flag is cleared).
1861 Check type 0x11 is CHECK_PTRACE_SCOPE (ptrace scope is restricted).
1862 Check type 0x12 is CHECK_ASLR (ASLR is enabled for the process).
1863 Check type 0x13 is CHECK_NX (NX bit is enabled for memory regions).
1864 Check type 0x14 is CHECK_STACK_CANARY (stack canary is present).
1865 Check type 0x15 is CHECK_FORTIFY (fortified functions are used).
1866 Check type 0x16 is CHECK_RELRO (RELRO is fully enabled).
1867 Check type 0x17 is CHECK_PIE (executable is position-independent).
1868 Check type 0x18 is CHECK_SUID (no setuid/setgid bits on executables).
1869 Check type 0x19 is CHECK_SYMLINK (no symlink traversal outside vault).
1870 Check type 0x1A is CHECK_HARDLINK (no hardlink creation across vault boundary).
1871 Check type 0x1B is CHECK_TMP (temporary files are within vault tmp directory).
1872 Check type 0x1C is CHECK_SHM (shared memory is within vault namespace).
1873 Check type 0x1D is CHECK_MQ (message queues are within vault namespace).
1874 Check type 0x1E is CHECK_SEM (semaphores are within vault namespace).
1875 Check type 0x1F is CHECK_ENV (environment variables do not contain secrets).
1876 Check type 0x20 is CHECK_ARGV (command line arguments do not contain secrets).
1877 Check type 0x21 is CHECK_STDIN (standard input does not contain secrets).
1878 Check type 0x22 is CHECK_STDOUT (standard output does not leak secrets).
1879 Check type 0x23 is CHECK_STDERR (standard error does not leak secrets).
1880 Check type 0x24 is CHECK_FILES (output files do not contain secrets).
1881 Check type 0x25 is CHECK_AUDIT (audit trail does not contain secrets).
1882 Check type 0x26 is CHECK_MANIFEST (manifest does not contain secrets).
1883 Check type 0x27 is CHECK_NETWORK (no network sockets are open).
1884 Check type 0x28 is CHECK_LISTEN (no listening sockets exist).
1885 Check type 0x29 is CHECK_CONNECT (no connected external sockets exist).
1886 Check type 0x2A is CHECK_CREDENTIALS (no credentials are present in memory).
1887 Check type 0x2B is CHECK_TOKENS (no authentication tokens are present in memory).
1888 Check type 0x2C is CHECK_KEYS (no cryptographic keys are present in memory).
1889 Check type 0x2D is CHECK_PASSWORDS (no passwords are present in memory).
1890 Check type 0x2E is CHECK_PII (no personally identifiable information in output).
1891 Check type 0x2F is CHECK_PHI (no protected health information in output).
1892 Check type 0x30 is CHECK_PCI (no payment card information in output).
1893 Bytes 8-11 contain the check frequency in milliseconds.
1894 Bytes 12-15 contain the check last execution timestamp (64-bit high).
1895 Bytes 16-19 contain the check last execution timestamp (64-bit low).
1896 Bytes 20-23 contain the check last execution timestamp nanoseconds.
1897 Bytes 24-27 contain the check result.
1898 Check result 0x01 is RESULT_PASS.
1899 Check result 0x02 is RESULT_FAIL.
1900 Check result 0x03 is RESULT_WARN.
1901 Check result 0x04 is RESULT_SKIP.
1902 Check result 0x05 is RESULT_ERROR.
1903 Bytes 28-31 contain check flags.
1904 Bit 31 indicates whether the check is enabled (1) or disabled (0).
1905 Bit 30 indicates whether the check is fail-closed (1) or fail-open (0).
1906 Bit 29 indicates whether the check result is cached (1) or live (0).
1907 Bit 28 indicates whether the check requires root privileges (1) or not (0).
1908 Bits 27-0 are reserved and must be zero.
1909 The enforcement policy table contains one record per active policy.
1910 Each enforcement policy record is 32 bytes.
1911 Bytes 0-3 contain the policy index.
1912 Bytes 4-7 contain the policy type (matches JCL manifest policy types).
1913 Bytes 8-11 contain the policy enforcement level.
1914 Bytes 12-15 contain the policy violation count.
1915 Bytes 16-19 contain the policy last violation timestamp (64-bit high).
1916 Bytes 20-23 contain the policy last violation timestamp (64-bit low).
1917 Bytes 24-27 contain the policy violation limit.
1918 Bytes 28-31 contain policy flags.
1919 Bit 31 indicates whether the policy is active (1) or inactive (0).
1920 Bit 30 indicates whether the policy triggers containment (1) or not (0).
1921 Bits 29-0 are reserved and must be zero.
1922 The namespace table contains one record per sandbox namespace.
1923 Each namespace record is 24 bytes.
1924 Bytes 0-3 contain the namespace index.
1925 Bytes 4-7 contain the namespace type.
1926 Namespace type 0x01 is NS_MOUNT.
1927 Namespace type 0x02 is NS_UTS.
1928 Namespace type 0x03 is NS_IPC.
1929 Namespace type 0x04 is NS_PID.
1930 Namespace type 0x05 is NS_NETWORK.
1931 Namespace type 0x06 is NS_USER.
1932 Namespace type 0x07 is NS_CGROUP.
1933 Bytes 8-11 contain the namespace identifier (inode number of /proc/self/ns/ link).
1934 Bytes 12-15 contain the parent namespace identifier (0 if root namespace).
1935 Bytes 16-19 contain the namespace flags.
1936 Bit 31 indicates whether the namespace is active (1) or not (0).
1937 Bit 30 indicates whether the namespace is unshared (1) or inherited (0).
1938 Bits 29-0 are reserved and must be zero.
1939 Bytes 20-23 contain the namespace creation timestamp (64-bit high, truncated to 32 bits).
1940 The file descriptor check table contains one record per open FD.
1941 Each FD check record is 32 bytes.
1942 Bytes 0-3 contain the file descriptor number.
1943 Bytes 4-7 contain the FD type.
1944 FD type 0x01 is FD_FILE (regular file).
1945 FD type 0x02 is FD_DIRECTORY.
1946 FD type 0x03 is FD_PIPE.
1947 FD type 0x04 is FD_SOCKET (prohibited).
1948 FD type 0x05 is FD_ANON (anonymous mapping).
1949 FD type 0x06 is FD_SYMLINK.
1950 Bytes 8-11 contain the FD path offset in the string table.
1951 Bytes 12-15 contain the FD path length.
1952 Bytes 16-19 contain the FD access mode.
1953 Access mode 0x01 is O_RDONLY.
1954 Access mode 0x02 is O_WRONLY.
1955 Access mode 0x03 is O_RDWR.
1956 Bytes 20-23 contain the FD flags.
1957 Bit 31 indicates whether the FD is within vault (1) or outside (0).
1958 Bit 30 indicates whether the FD is allowed (1) or prohibited (0).
1959 Bits 29-0 are reserved and must be zero.
1960 Bytes 24-27 contain the FD inode number.
1961 Bytes 28-31 contain the FD device number.
1962 The memory region check table contains one record per memory mapping.
1963 Each memory region record is 48 bytes.
1964 Bytes 0-7 contain the region start address (64-bit).
1965 Bytes 8-15 contain the region end address (64-bit).
1966 Bytes 16-23 contain the region size in bytes.
1967 Bytes 24-27 contain the region protection flags.
1968 Protection bit 0 is PROT_READ (0x01).
1969 Protection bit 1 is PROT_WRITE (0x02).
1970 Protection bit 2 is PROT_EXEC (0x04).
1971 Protection bit 3 is PROT_NONE (0x00).
1972 Bytes 28-31 contain the region mapping type.
1973 Mapping type 0x01 is MAP_FILE (backed by file).
1974 Mapping type 0x02 is MAP_ANONYMOUS (not backed by file).
1975 Mapping type 0x03 is MAP_STACK (thread stack).
1976 Mapping type 0x04 is MAP_HEAP (process heap).
1977 Bytes 32-35 contain the backing file path offset in the string table.
1978 Bytes 36-39 contain the backing file path length.
1979 Bytes 40-43 contain the region flags.
1980 Bit 31 indicates whether the region is within vault (1) or outside (0).
1981 Bit 30 indicates whether the region is executable (1) or not (0).
1982 Bit 29 indicates whether the region is writable (1) or not (0).
1983 Bit 28 indicates whether the region is readable (1) or not (0).
1984 Bits 27-0 are reserved and must be zero.
1985 Bytes 44-47 contain the region inode number (0 if anonymous).
1986 The network endpoint check table contains one record per socket.
1987 Each network endpoint record is 32 bytes.
1988 Bytes 0-3 contain the socket file descriptor.
1989 Bytes 4-7 contain the socket domain.
1990 Domain 0x01 is AF_UNIX.
1991 Domain 0x02 is AF_INET.
1992 Domain 0x03 is AF_INET6.
1993 Domain 0x04 is AF_NETLINK.
1994 Domain 0x05 is AF_PACKET.
1995 Bytes 8-11 contain the socket type.
1996 Type 0x01 is SOCK_STREAM.
1997 Type 0x02 is SOCK_DGRAM.
1998 Type 0x03 is SOCK_RAW.
1999 Type 0x04 is SOCK_SEQPACKET.
2000 Bytes 12-15 contain the socket protocol.
2001 Protocol 0x00 is IPPROTO_IP (default).
2002 Protocol 0x01 is IPPROTO_ICMP.
2003 Protocol 0x06 is IPPROTO_TCP.
2004 Protocol 0x11 is IPPROTO_UDP.
2005 Bytes 16-19 contain the local address (IPv4 as 32-bit integer, or first 4 bytes of IPv6).
2006 Bytes 20-23 contain the local port (16-bit, padded to 32 bits).
2007 Bytes 24-27 contain the remote address (same encoding as local).
2008 Bytes 28-31 contain the remote port (16-bit, padded to 32 bits).
2009 Any socket with domain AF_INET or AF_INET6 triggers a security boundary failure.
2010 Any socket with domain AF_NETLINK triggers a security boundary failure.
2011 Any socket with domain AF_PACKET triggers a security boundary failure.
2012 AF_UNIX sockets are permitted only if the path is within the vault.
2013 The capability check table contains one record per process capability.
2014 Each capability record is 16 bytes.
2015 Bytes 0-3 contain the capability index.
2016 Bytes 4-7 contain the capability identifier.
2017 Capability 0x00 is CAP_CHOWN.
2018 Capability 0x01 is CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE.
2019 Capability 0x02 is CAP_DAC_READ_SEARCH.
2020 Capability 0x03 is CAP_FOWNER.
2021 Capability 0x04 is CAP_FSETID.
2022 Capability 0x05 is CAP_KILL.
2023 Capability 0x06 is CAP_SETGID.
2024 Capability 0x07 is CAP_SETUID.
2025 Capability 0x08 is CAP_SETPCAP.
2026 Capability 0x09 is CAP_LINUX_IMMUTABLE.
2027 Capability 0x0A is CAP_NET_BIND_SERVICE.
2028 Capability 0x0B is CAP_NET_BROADCAST.
2029 Capability 0x0C is CAP_NET_ADMIN.
2030 Capability 0x0D is CAP_NET_RAW.
2031 Capability 0x0E is CAP_IPC_LOCK.
2032 Capability 0x0F is CAP_IPC_OWNER.
2033 Capability 0x10 is CAP_SYS_MODULE.
2034 Capability 0x11 is CAP_SYS_RAWIO.
2035 Capability 0x12 is CAP_SYS_CHROOT.
2036 Capability 0x13 is CAP_SYS_PTRACE.
2037 Capability 0x14 is CAP_SYS_PACCT.
2038 Capability 0x15 is CAP_SYS_ADMIN.
2039 Capability 0x16 is CAP_SYS_BOOT.
2040 Capability 0x17 is CAP_SYS_NICE.
2041 Capability 0x18 is CAP_SYS_RESOURCE.
2042 Capability 0x19 is CAP_SYS_TIME.
2043 Capability 0x1A is CAP_SYS_TTY_CONFIG.
2044 Capability 0x1B is CAP_MKNOD.
2045 Capability 0x1C is CAP_LEASE.
2046 Capability 0x1D is CAP_AUDIT_WRITE.
2047 Capability 0x1E is CAP_AUDIT_CONTROL.
2048 Capability 0x1F is CAP_SETFCAP.
2049 Capability 0x20 is CAP_MAC_OVERRIDE.
2050 Capability 0x21 is CAP_MAC_ADMIN.
2051 Capability 0x22 is CAP_SYSLOG.
2052 Capability 0x23 is CAP_WAKE_ALARM.
2053 Capability 0x24 is CAP_BLOCK_SUSPEND.
2054 Capability 0x25 is CAP_AUDIT_READ.
2055 Capability 0x26 is CAP_PERFMON.
2056 Capability 0x27 is CAP_BPF.
2057 Capability 0x28 is CAP_CHECKPOINT_RESTORE.
2058 Bytes 8-11 contain the capability effective flag (0x00 or 0x01).
2059 Bytes 12-15 contain the capability permitted flag (0x00 or 0x01).
2060 Bytes 16-19 contain the capability inheritable flag (0x00 or 0x01).
2061 Bytes 20-23 contain the capability bounding set flag (0x00 or 0x01).
2062 All capabilities except CAP_NET_BIND_SERVICE must be dropped in the sandbox.
2063 CAP_NET_BIND_SERVICE is permitted only if explicitly requested in the manifest.
2064 Any non-dropped capability triggers a security boundary failure.
2065 The syscall whitelist is encoded as an array of 32-bit syscall numbers.
2066 The whitelist array follows the capability check table in the security boundary file.
2067 Syscall numbers not in the whitelist trigger a seccomp violation.
2068 The seccomp filter is a Berkeley Packet Filter (BPF) program.
2069 The BPF program is validated for termination before loading.
2070 The BPF program is validated for memory safety before loading.
2071 The BPF program is validated to contain only allowed opcodes.
2072 Allowed BPF opcodes are: BPF_LD, BPF_LDX, BPF_ST, BPF_STX, BPF_ALU, BPF_JMP, BPF_RET, BPF_MISC.
2073 The BPF program maximum length is 4096 instructions.
2074 The BPF program uses BPF_JMP for conditional branching on syscall numbers.
2075 The BPF program uses BPF_RET with SECCOMP_RET_ALLOW for whitelisted syscalls.
2076 The BPF program uses BPF_RET with SECCOMP_RET_ERRNO for denied syscalls.
2077 The BPF program uses BPF_RET with SECCOMP_RET_KILL for critical violations.
2078 The BPF program does not use SECCOMP_RET_TRAP.
2079 The BPF program does not use SECCOMP_RET_TRACE.
2080 The BPF program does not use SECCOMP_RET_LOG as the sole response.
2081 The security boundary file is updated in real-time during job execution.
2082 The security boundary file is read-only to the job processes.
2083 The security boundary file is writable only by the sandbox monitor process.
2084 The sandbox monitor process runs outside the sandbox namespace.
2085 The sandbox monitor process has elevated privileges for boundary enforcement.
2086 The sandbox monitor process does not execute user code.
2087 The sandbox monitor process validates its own integrity before starting.
2088 The sandbox monitor process hash is recorded in the audit manifest.
2089 The security boundary verification runs at the configured interval.
2090 The verification interval default is 1000 milliseconds.
2091 The verification interval minimum is 100 milliseconds.
2092 The verification interval maximum is 60000 milliseconds.
2093 Verification results are written to the security audit log.
2094 The security audit log uses magic 0x53454155 ('SEAU').
2095 The security audit log header is 16 bytes standard plus 16 bytes of log metadata.
2096 Bytes 16-19 contain the number of log entries.
2097 Bytes 20-23 contain the log entry table offset.
2098 Bytes 24-27 contain the maximum log entries (circular buffer size).
2099 Bytes 28-31 contain the log wrap count (how many times the buffer wrapped).
2100 Each security audit log entry is 32 bytes.
2101 Bytes 0-3 contain the entry index.
2102 Bytes 4-7 contain the entry timestamp (64-bit Unix epoch seconds, high 32 bits).
2103 Bytes 8-11 contain the entry timestamp (64-bit low).
2104 Bytes 12-15 contain the entry timestamp nanoseconds.
2105 Bytes 16-19 contain the entry type.
2106 Entry type 0x01 is LOG_CHECK_PASS.
2107 Entry type 0x02 is LOG_CHECK_FAIL.
2108 Entry type 0x03 is LOG_CHECK_WARN.
2109 Entry type 0x04 is LOG_POLICY_VIOLATION.
2110 Entry type 0x05 is LOG_CONTAINMENT_ACTION.
2111 Entry type 0x06 is LOG_SYSCALL_DENY.
2112 Entry type 0x07 is LOG_FD_VIOLATION.
2113 Entry type 0x08 is LOG_MMAP_VIOLATION.
2114 Entry type 0x09 is LOG_NS_VIOLATION.
2115 Entry type 0x0A is LOG_CAP_VIOLATION.
2116 Entry type 0x0B is LOG_CREDENTIAL_LEAK.
2117 Entry type 0x0C is LOG_NETWORK_VIOLATION.
2118 Entry type 0x0D is LOG_VERIFICATION_DRIFT.
2119 Bytes 20-23 contain the entry severity.
2120 Bytes 24-27 contain the related check index (0xFFFFFFFF if none).
2121 Bytes 28-31 contain the entry flags.
2122 Bit 31 indicates whether the entry requires immediate attention (1) or not (0).
2123 Bits 30-0 are reserved and must be zero.
2124 The security boundary file hash is SHA-256 over the complete file.
2125 The security boundary file hash is recorded in the audit manifest.
2126 The security boundary is fail-closed: any verification failure triggers containment.
2127 The security boundary does not allow graceful degradation on critical failures.
2128 The security boundary treats all job processes as potentially hostile.
2129 The security boundary assumes the sandbox monitor itself may be compromised.
2130 The security boundary uses multiple independent verification channels.
2131 Channel 1 is the in-process seccomp filter.
2132 Channel 2 is the out-of-process sandbox monitor.
2133 Channel 3 is the kernel audit subsystem.
2134 Channel 4 is the file system integrity checks.
2135 Disagreement between channels triggers an immediate containment action.
2136 The security boundary does not trust the job's own reporting of its state.
2137 The security boundary verifies state through external observation.
2138 External observation includes /proc inspection, ptrace-free status checks, and kernel audit logs.
2139 The security boundary does not use ptrace on sandboxed processes.
2140 The security boundary does not rely on cooperative process behavior.
