2488 Bytes 20-23 contain the artifact hash match status (0=VERIFIED, 1=MISSING, 2=MISMATCH).
2489 Bytes 24-27 contain the artifact validation status (0=PASS, 1=FAIL, 2=WARN, 3=SKIP).
2490 Bytes 28-31 contain the artifact processing time in milliseconds.
2491 The generated artifact summary table has identical structure.
2492 The validation phase summary table contains V records of 32 bytes each.
2493 Bytes 0-3 contain the phase index.
2494 Bytes 4-7 contain the phase identifier (matches validation phase enumeration).
2495 Bytes 8-11 contain the phase start timestamp (64-bit high).
2496 Bytes 12-15 contain the phase start timestamp (64-bit low).
2497 Bytes 16-19 contain the phase end timestamp (64-bit high).
2498 Bytes 20-23 contain the phase end timestamp (64-bit low).
2499 Bytes 24-27 contain the phase status (0=PASS, 1=FAIL, 2=WARN, 3=SKIP, 4=ABORT).
2500 Bytes 28-31 contain the number of checks in the phase.
2501 The record size is 32 bytes, bytes 0-31 are used.
2502 The test case summary table contains T records of 32 bytes each.
2503 Bytes 0-3 contain the test case index.
2504 Bytes 4-7 contain the test case type.
2505 Bytes 8-11 contain the test case name offset in the string table.
2506 Bytes 12-15 contain the test case name length.
2507 Bytes 16-19 contain the expected status.
2508 Bytes 20-23 contain the actual status.
2509 Bytes 24-27 contain the execution time in milliseconds.
2510 Bytes 28-31 contain the test case flags.
2511 The policy violation summary table contains P records of 32 bytes each.
2512 Bytes 0-3 contain the violation index.
2513 Bytes 4-7 contain the policy type.
2514 Bytes 8-11 contain the enforcement action.
2515 Bytes 12-15 contain the step index.
2516 Bytes 16-19 contain the violation timestamp (64-bit high).
2517 Bytes 20-23 contain the violation timestamp (64-bit low).
2518 Bytes 24-27 contain the violation description offset in the string table.
2519 Bytes 28-31 contain the violation description length.
2520 The complexity claim summary table contains C records of 32 bytes each.
2521 Bytes 0-3 contain the claim index.
2522 Bytes 4-7 contain the claim phase.
2523 Bytes 8-11 contain the claim status.
2524 Bytes 12-15 contain the polynomial degree.
2525 Bytes 16-23 contain the constant c (IEEE 754 binary64).
2526 Bytes 24-27 contain the input encoding type.
2527 Bytes 28-31 contain the claim justification offset in the string table.
2528 The security check summary table contains B records of 32 bytes each.
2529 Bytes 0-3 contain the check index.
2530 Bytes 4-7 contain the check type.
2531 Bytes 8-11 contain the check result.
2532 Bytes 12-15 contain the check last execution timestamp (64-bit high).
2533 Bytes 16-19 contain the check last execution timestamp (64-bit low).
2534 Bytes 20-23 contain the check failure count.
2535 Bytes 24-27 contain the check containment action count.
2536 Bytes 28-31 contain the check flags.
2537 The conformance report string table follows the same rules as previous string tables.
2538 The conformance report string table contains artifact names, paths, descriptions, and justifications.
2539 The conformance report is generated as the final step of the pipeline.
2540 The conformance report is not generated if the pipeline aborts before the audit phase.
2541 If the pipeline aborts, a partial conformance report is generated with status CONFORMANCE_ABORT.
2542 The partial conformance report includes all data available up to the abort point.
2543 The conformance report is validated for structural integrity.
2544 Conformance report validation checks magic, version, header checksum, and payload length.
2545 Conformance report validation checks that all table offsets are within payload bounds.
2546 Conformance report validation checks that all string table offsets are within bounds.
2547 Conformance report validation checks that artifact counts match table sizes.
2548 Conformance report validation checks that the overall status is consistent with phase statuses.
2549 If any phase has status FAIL, the overall status must be CONFORMANCE_FAIL or CONFORMANCE_ABORT.
2550 If any phase has status ABORT, the overall status must be CONFORMANCE_ABORT.
2551 If all phases have status PASS and no warnings, the overall status must be CONFORMANCE_PASS.
2552 If all phases have status PASS but warnings exist, the overall status must be CONFORMANCE_WARN.
2553 If the security state is STATE_BREACHED, the overall status must be CONFORMANCE_SECURITY.
2554 The conformance report file hash is SHA-256 over the complete file.
2555 The conformance report file hash is recorded in the audit manifest as the final artifact.
2556 The conformance report does not contain credentials.
2557 The conformance report does not contain secret values.
2558 The conformance report does not contain network addresses.
2559 The conformance report does not contain file paths outside the vault.
2560 The conformance report is deterministic: identical pipeline execution produces identical report bytes.
2561 The conformance report generation does not use randomness.
2562 The conformance report generation records its version in the report header.
2563 The conformance report is the primary output for human review.
2564 The conformance report is accompanied by the machine-readable audit trail.
2565 The conformance report and audit trail together provide complete provenance.
2566 The conformance report is fail-closed: any structural validation failure aborts report generation.
2567 The conformance report is not published if the audit trail hash does not match the recorded hash.
2568 The conformance report is not published if any required artifact is missing.
2569 The conformance report is not published if any artifact hash mismatch is detected.
2570 The conformance report is not published if the security state is not STATE_SECURE or STATE_RECOVERED.
2571 The conformance report publication requires explicit approval from the validation protocol.
2572 The validation protocol must have a rule of type RULE_HASH that verifies all artifact hashes.
2573 The validation protocol must have a rule of type RULE_SANDBOX that verifies the security state.
2574 The validation protocol must have a rule of type RULE_CREDENTIAL that verifies no credential leakage.
2575 The validation protocol must have a rule of type RULE_DETERMINISM that verifies output determinism.
2576 The validation protocol must have a rule of type RULE_COMPLEXITY that verifies complexity claims.
2577 The validation protocol must have a rule of type RULE_SEMANTIC that verifies semantic equivalence.
2578 The validation protocol must have a rule of type RULE_PROVENANCE that verifies audit trail completeness.
2579 The conformance report contains a signature block if the signed flag is set.
2580 The signature block is 256 bytes containing an RSA-PSS signature over the report hash.
2581 The RSA key size is 2048 bits.
2582 The RSA-PSS salt length is 32 bytes.
2583 The RSA-PSS hash function is SHA-256.
2584 The signature block follows the payload and is not included in the payload length.
2585 The signature block is included in the file hash computation.
2586 The signing key is stored in a hardware security module outside the vault.
2587 The signing key public component is recorded in the vault configuration.
2588 The signing key hash is recorded in the audit manifest.
2589 The signature verification uses the public key from the vault configuration.
2590 Signature verification failure triggers a validation warning.
2591 The conformance report is the final artifact of the pipeline.
2592 No artifacts are generated after the conformance report.
2593 The pipeline execution is atomic: either all artifacts are generated and validated, or none are published.
2594 Partial artifact publication is prohibited.
2595 Artifact publication occurs only after the conformance report is generated and validated.
2596 The publication mechanism copies artifacts from the vault workspace to the vault output directory.
2597 The output directory is a subdirectory of the vault root.
2598 The output directory path is specified in the JCL manifest.
2599 The output directory is created with permissions 0o700 (rwx------).
2600 The output directory is validated for vault containment before use.

SECTION 0013: DETERMINISM AND REPRODUCIBILITY BINARY GUARANTEES
================================================================================

2601 Every artifact in the pipeline must be deterministic.
2602 Determinism means that identical inputs produce identical byte-level outputs.
2603 The determinism guarantee applies to all artifact types.
2604 The determinism guarantee applies to the token stream file.
2605 The determinism guarantee applies to the PTM-IR file.
2606 The determinism guarantee applies to the CFG file.
2607 The determinism guarantee applies to the PTM transition table file.
2608 The determinism guarantee applies to the complexity report file.
2609 The determinism guarantee applies to the Quipper artifact file.
2610 The determinism guarantee applies to the JCL manifest file.
2611 The determinism guarantee applies to the audit trail file.
2612 The determinism guarantee applies to the test vector file.
2613 The determinism guarantee applies to the security boundary file.
2614 The determinism guarantee applies to the conformance report file.
2615 The determinism guarantee applies to the validation protocol file.
2616 The determinism guarantee applies to the diagnostic log file.
2617 The determinism guarantee applies to the snapshot file.
2618 The determinism guarantee applies to the OpenQASM translation file.
2619 Determinism is verified by the TEST_DETERMINISM test case.
2620 The TEST_DETERMINISM test case executes the pipeline twice with identical inputs.
2621 The TEST_DETERMINISM test case compares the SHA-256 hashes of all artifacts.
2622 All artifact hashes must match exactly between the two executions.
2623 Any hash mismatch triggers a validation failure.
2624 Hash mismatch indicates a non-deterministic component in the pipeline.
2625 Non-deterministic components must be identified and eliminated.
2626 Common sources of non-determinism include: timestamps in artifacts, random numbers, pointer addresses, iteration order over hash tables, file system ordering, process IDs, thread scheduling.
2627 Timestamps in artifacts are prohibited except in the audit trail and conformance report.
2628 The audit trail timestamps are obtained from the environment clock and are expected to differ between runs.
2629 The audit trail is excluded from the determinism hash comparison.
2630 The conformance report timestamps are also excluded from the determinism hash comparison.
2631 All other artifacts must not contain timestamps, process IDs, or other runtime-dependent values.
2632 Random numbers are prohibited in all pipeline stages except where explicitly required for quantum simulation.
2633 Quantum simulation randomness uses a fixed seed of zero.
2634 The fixed seed is recorded in the audit manifest.
2635 Pointer addresses are prohibited in artifacts.
2636 All offsets in artifacts are relative to structure starts, not absolute memory addresses.
2637 Hash table iteration order is prohibited from affecting artifact output.
2638 Hash tables used for deduplication must sort their output by hash value before serialization.
2639 The sort order is ascending unsigned 32-bit integer.
2640 File system directory iteration order is prohibited from affecting artifact output.
2641 Input files are processed in lexicographic order of their paths.
2642 The lexicographic order uses ASCII byte comparison.
2643 Thread scheduling is prohibited from affecting artifact output.
2644 The pipeline is single-threaded for all artifact generation stages.
2645 Parallel execution is permitted only for independent validation checks.
2646 Parallel validation checks must not write to shared artifacts.
2647 The reproducibility guarantee requires that all configuration assets are recorded.
2648 Configuration assets include: seccomp filter, keyword table, EBCDIC table, error code table, vault policy, agent parameters.
2649 Each configuration asset hash is recorded in the audit manifest.
2650 The reproducibility guarantee requires that the agent version is recorded.
2651 The agent version is recorded in the audit manifest and conformance report.
2652 The reproducibility guarantee requires that the host identifier is recorded.
2653 The host identifier is a hash of the hostname, not the raw hostname.
2654 The reproducibility guarantee requires that the vault path is recorded.
2655 The vault path is recorded in the audit manifest.
2656 The reproducibility guarantee requires that the execution user and group are recorded.
2657 The execution user and group are recorded in the JCL manifest and audit trail.
2658 The reproducibility guarantee requires that the umask is recorded.
2659 The umask is recorded in the JCL manifest.
2660 The reproducibility guarantee requires that resource limits are recorded.
2661 Resource limits are recorded in the JCL manifest.
2662 The reproducibility guarantee requires that security policies are recorded.
2663 Security policies are recorded in the JCL manifest and security boundary file.
2664 The reproducibility guarantee requires that environment variables are recorded.
2665 Environment variables are recorded in the JCL manifest and audit trail.
2666 Secret environment variables are masked but their presence is recorded.
2667 The reproducibility guarantee requires that the input source file is preserved.
2668 The input source file is preserved as a read-only source artifact.
2669 The reproducibility guarantee requires that all intermediate artifacts are preserved.
2670 Intermediate artifacts are preserved as read-only generated artifacts.
2671 The reproducibility guarantee requires that the pipeline can be re-executed with identical results.
2672 Re-execution uses the recorded configuration assets and identical inputs.
2673 Re-execution produces artifacts with identical SHA-256 hashes.
2674 The determinism and reproducibility guarantees are validated by the TEST_DETERMINISM and TEST_HASH_STABILITY test cases.
2675 The TEST_HASH_STABILITY test case computes the artifact hash 100 times in a single execution.
2676 All 100 hash computations must produce identical results.
2677 Hash stability failure indicates a race condition or non-deterministic state mutation.
2678 The determinism guarantee is a critical requirement.
2679 Violation of the determinism guarantee triggers a validation failure.
2680 The validation failure is classified as SEV_FATAL.
2681 The pipeline aborts on determinism guarantee violation.
2682 The determinism guarantee is documented in the conformance report.
2683 The conformance report includes a section stating whether determinism was verified.
2684 The determinism verification status is recorded in the conformance report flags.
2685 Bit 27 of the conformance report flags indicates determinism verified (1) or not (0).
2686 Bit 26 of the conformance report flags indicates reproducibility verified (1) or not (0).
2687 Bits 25-0 are reserved and must be zero.
2688 The determinism guarantee extends to the binary level: every bit must be identical.
2689 Byte-level comparison is insufficient; bit-level identity is required.
2690 Padding bits must be zero and identical across runs.
2691 Padding bytes must be zero and identical across runs.
2692 Alignment gaps must be filled with zeros and identical across runs.
2693 The determinism guarantee is enforced by the validation protocol rule RULE_DETERMINISM.
2694 The RULE_DETERMINISM check function executes the pipeline twice and compares hashes.
2695 The RULE_DETERMINISM check function uses the validation bytecode OP_HASH instruction.
2696 The RULE_DETERMINISM check function stores the first execution hashes in temporary storage.
2697 The RULE_DETERMINISM check function compares the second execution hashes using OP_CMP.
2698 The RULE_DETERMINISM check function returns 0 (PASS) only if all hashes match.
2699 The RULE_DETERMINISM check function returns a non-zero error code if any hash mismatches.
2700 The determinism and reproducibility binary guarantees are foundational to the audit trail.

SECTION 0014: ERROR HANDLING AND RECOVERY BINARY PROTOCOLS
================================================================================

2701 The error handling protocol defines binary formats for all error conditions.
2702 The error handling file uses magic 0x45525248 ('ERRH').
2703 The error handling header is 16 bytes standard plus 32 bytes of error metadata.
2704 Bytes 16-19 contain the error protocol version (0x00000001).
2705 Bytes 20-23 contain the number of error categories.
2706 Bytes 24-27 contain the number of error handlers.
2707 Bytes 28-31 contain the number of recovery procedures.
2708 Bytes 32-35 contain the error category table offset.
2709 Bytes 36-39 contain the error handler table offset.
2710 Bytes 40-43 contain the recovery procedure table offset.
2711 Bytes 44-47 contain the number of fatal errors that occurred.
2712 Bytes 48-51 contain the number of non-fatal errors that occurred.
2713 Bytes 52-55 contain the last error code.
2714 Bytes 56-59 contain the last error timestamp (64-bit Unix epoch seconds, high 32 bits).
2715 Bytes 60-63 contain the last error timestamp (64-bit low 32 bits).
2716 The error category table contains one record per category.
2717 Each error category record is 24 bytes.
2718 Bytes 0-3 contain the category index.
2719 Bytes 4-7 contain the category identifier.
2720 Category 0x01 is CAT_LEXICAL (lexical analysis errors).
2721 Category 0x02 is CAT_SYNTACTIC (parsing errors).
2722 Category 0x03 is CAT_SEMANTIC (semantic analysis errors).
2723 Category 0x04 is CAT_TYPE (type system errors).
2724 Category 0x05 is CAT_CONTROL_FLOW (control flow graph errors).
2725 Category 0x06 is CAT_PTM (Turing machine lowering errors).
2726 Category 0x07 is CAT_COMPLEXITY (complexity analysis errors).
2727 Category 0x08 is CAT_QUIPPER (quantum circuit generation errors).
2728 Category 0x09 is CAT_JCL (manifest validation errors).
2729 Category 0x0A is CAT_AUDIT (audit trail errors).
2730 Category 0x0B is CAT_VALIDATION (validation protocol errors).
2731 Category 0x0C is CAT_TEST (test execution errors).
2732 Category 0x0D is CAT_SECURITY (security boundary errors).
2733 Category 0x0E is CAT_SANDBOX (sandbox enforcement errors).
2734 Category 0x0F is CAT_RESOURCE (resource limit errors).
2735 Category 0x10 is CAT_IO (input/output errors).
2736 Category 0x11 is CAT_NETWORK (network access errors).
2737 Category 0x12 is CAT_MEMORY (memory allocation errors).
2738 Category 0x13 is CAT_DISK (disk space errors).
2739 Category 0x14 is CAT_PERMISSION (permission denied errors).
2740 Category 0x15 is CAT_NOT_FOUND (file not found errors).
2741 Category 0x16 is CAT_ALREADY_EXISTS (file already exists errors).
2742 Category 0x17 is CAT_TIMEOUT (timeout errors).
2743 Category 0x18 is CAT_INTERRUPT (interruption errors).
2744 Category 0x19 is CAT_ASSERTION (internal assertion failures).
2745 Category 0x1A is CAT_INVARIANT (invariant violation errors).
2746 Category 0x1B is CAT_DETERMINISM (determinism violation errors).
2747 Category 0x1C is CAT_REPRODUCIBILITY (reproducibility violation errors).
2748 Category 0x1D is CAT_HASH_MISMATCH (hash mismatch errors).
2749 Category 0x1E is CAT_VERSION_MISMATCH (version incompatibility errors).
2750 Category 0x1F is CAT_MAGIC_MISMATCH (magic number mismatch errors).
2751 Category 0x20 is CAT_CHECKSUM_FAIL (checksum verification errors).
2752 Category 0x21 is CAT_ENCODING_INVALID (character encoding errors).
2753 Category 0x22 is CAT_ALIGNMENT (memory alignment errors).
2754 Category 0x23 is CAT_RESERVED_BITS (reserved bit violation errors).
2755 Category 0x24 is CAT_ORACLE_ASSUMPTION (unstated oracle errors).
2756 Category 0x25 is CAT_UNITARY (non-unitary matrix errors).
2757 Category 0x26 is CAT_CYCLE (cyclic dependency errors).
2758 Category 0x27 is CAT_CREDENTIAL_LEAK (credential leakage errors).
2759 Category 0x28 is CAT_SECRET_LEAK (secret leakage errors).
2760 Category 0x29 is CAT_SANDBOX_ESCAPE (sandbox boundary escape errors).
2761 Category 0x2A is CAT_ARBITRARY_COMMAND (arbitrary command execution errors).
2762 Category 0x2B is CAT_CREDENTIAL_INFERENCE (credential inference errors).
2763 Category 0x2C is CAT_HARD_CODED (hard-coded result errors).
2764 Category 0x2D is CAT_OPENQASM_PRIMARY (OpenQASM as primary output errors).
2765 Category 0x2E is CAT_LAYER_COLLAPSE (layer collapse errors).
2766 Category 0x2F is CAT_SEMANTIC_CHANGE (semantic alteration errors).
2767 Category 0x30 is CAT_MISSING_PROVENANCE (missing provenance errors).
2768 Category 0x31 is CAT_TIMESTAMP_FABRICATED (fabricated timestamp errors).
2769 Category 0x32 is CAT_UNKNOWN (unclassified errors).
2770 Bytes 8-11 contain the category error count.
2771 Bytes 12-15 contain the category fatal count.
2772 Bytes 16-19 contain the category warning count.
2773 Bytes 20-23 contain the category flags.
2774 Bit 31 indicates whether the category is fail-closed (1) or fail-open (0).
2775 Bit 30 indicates whether the category triggers containment (1) or not (0).
2776 Bits 29-0 are reserved and must be zero.
2777 The error handler table contains one record per handler.
2778 Each error handler record is 32 bytes.
2779 Bytes 0-3 contain the handler index.
2780 Bytes 4-7 contain the handler category.
2781 Bytes 8-11 contain the handler trigger condition.
2782 Condition 0x01 is TRIGGER_ALWAYS (always execute this handler).
2783 Condition 0x02 is TRIGGER_FATAL (execute only for fatal errors).
2784 Condition 0x03 is TRIGGER_NONFATAL (execute only for non-fatal errors).
2785 Condition 0x04 is TRIGGER_SPECIFIC (execute only for specific error codes).
2786 Bytes 12-15 contain the specific error code (valid only if condition is TRIGGER_SPECIFIC).
2787 Bytes 16-19 contain the handler action.
2788 Action 0x01 is ACTION_LOG (write to diagnostic log).
2789 Action 0x02 is ACTION_AUDIT (write to audit trail).
2790 Action 0x03 is ACTION_ABORT (abort the current phase).
2791 Action 0x04 is ACTION_KILL (kill the current process).
2792 Action 0x05 is ACTION_RETRY (retry the operation up to N times).
2793 Action 0x06 is ACTION_SKIP (skip the current operation and continue).
2794 Action 0x07 is ACTION_ROLLBACK (rollback to last checkpoint).
2795 Action 0x08 is ACTION_ISOLATE (isolate the process in a restricted namespace).
2796 Action 0x09 is ACTION_NOTIFY (send notification to external monitor).
2797 Action 0x0A is ACTION_HALT (halt the entire pipeline).
2798 Bytes 20-23 contain the handler retry count (valid only if action is ACTION_RETRY).
2799 Bytes 24-27 contain the handler timeout in milliseconds (0 for no timeout).
2800 Bytes 28-31 contain handler flags.
2801 Bit 31 indicates whether the handler is enabled (1) or disabled (0).
2802 Bit 30 indicates whether the handler is synchronous (1) or asynchronous (0).
2803 Bit 29 indicates whether the handler requires elevated privileges (1) or not (0).
2804 Bits 28-0 are reserved and must be zero.
2805 The recovery procedure table contains one record per recovery procedure.
2806 Each recovery procedure record is 48 bytes.
2807 Bytes 0-3 contain the recovery index.
2808 Bytes 4-7 contain the recovery trigger category.
2809 Bytes 8-11 contain the recovery trigger error code (0xFFFFFFFF for any error in category).
2810 Bytes 12-15 contain the recovery type.
2811 Recovery type 0x01 is RECOVERY_RESTART_PHASE (restart the current phase from beginning).
2812 Recovery type 0x02 is RECOVERY_RESTART_STEP (restart the current job step).
2813 Recovery type 0x03 is RECOVERY_CONTINUE (continue with degraded functionality).
2814 Recovery type 0x04 is RECOVERY_SKIP_PHASE (skip the current phase and proceed).
2815 Recovery type 0x05 is RECOVERY_SKIP_STEP (skip the current step and proceed).
2816 Recovery type 0x06 is RECOVERY_USE_CHECKPOINT (restore from last checkpoint).
2817 Recovery type 0x07 is RECOVERY_USE_DEFAULT (use default values for missing data).
2818 Recovery type 0x08 is RECOVERY_USE_ALTERNATE (use alternate implementation).
2819 Recovery type 0x09 is RECOVERY_REQUEST_INPUT (request human intervention).
2820 Recovery type 0x0A is RECOVERY_TERMINATE (terminate the pipeline gracefully).
2821 Bytes 16-19 contain the recovery checkpoint offset (valid only for RECOVERY_USE_CHECKPOINT).
2822 Bytes 20-23 contain the recovery max attempts.
2823 Bytes 24-27 contain the recovery delay between attempts in milliseconds.
2824 Bytes 28-31 contain the recovery success criteria.
2825 Criteria 0x01 is CRITERIA_NO_ERROR (no error occurs during recovery).
2826 Criteria 0x02 is CRITERIA_VALID_OUTPUT (output passes validation).
2827 Criteria 0x03 is CRITERIA_PARTIAL_OUTPUT (at least partial output is produced).
2828 Criteria 0x04 is CRITERIA_TIMEOUT (recovery completes within timeout).
2829 Bytes 32-35 contain the recovery timeout in milliseconds.
2830 Bytes 36-39 contain the recovery description offset in the string table.
2831 Bytes 40-43 contain the recovery description length.
2832 Bytes 44-47 contain recovery flags.
2833 Bit 31 indicates whether the recovery is enabled (1) or disabled (0).
2834 Bit 30 indicates whether the recovery is automatic (1) or manual (0).
2835 Bits 29-0 are reserved and must be zero.
2836 The error handling protocol is fail-closed by default.
2837 Unhandled errors in fail-closed categories trigger immediate pipeline abort.
2838 The error handling protocol does not silently ignore errors.
2839 The error handling protocol does not replace unknown errors with guessed results.
2840 The error handling protocol records every error in the diagnostic log.
2841 The error handling protocol records fatal errors in the audit trail.
2842 The error handling protocol does not leak credentials in error messages.
2843 The error handling protocol masks secret values in error messages.
2844 The error handling protocol does not include stack traces in production artifacts.
2845 Stack traces are written to the diagnostic log only.
2846 The error handling protocol uses structured error codes.
2847 Error codes are 32-bit integers with the following layout: bits 31-24 are the category, bits 23-16 are the severity, bits 15-0 are the specific code.
2848 Category 0x01 (lexical) with severity 0x03 (error) and code 0x0001 is LEXICAL_INVALID_CHARACTER.
2849 Category 0x01 with severity 0x03 and code 0x0002 is LEXICAL_UNTERMINATED_LITERAL.
2850 Category 0x01 with severity 0x03 and code 0x0003 is LEXICAL_LINE_TOO_LONG.
2851 Category 0x02 with severity 0x03 and code 0x0001 is SYNTACTIC_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN.
2852 Category 0x02 with severity 0x03 and code 0x0002 is SYNTACTIC_MISSING_DELIMITER.
2853 Category 0x03 with severity 0x03 and code 0x0001 is SEMANTIC_UNDECLARED_VARIABLE.
2854 Category 0x03 with severity 0x03 and code 0x0002 is SEMANTIC_MULTIPLE_DECLARATION.
2855 Category 0x04 with severity 0x03 and code 0x0001 is TYPE_INCOMPATIBLE_ASSIGNMENT.
2856 Category 0x04 with severity 0x03 and code 0x0002 is TYPE_INCOMPATIBLE_OPERATION.
2857 Category 0x05 with severity 0x03 and code 0x0001 is CFG_UNREACHABLE_BLOCK.
2858 Category 0x05 with severity 0x03 and code 0x0002 is CFG_CYCLE_DETECTED.
2859 Category 0x06 with severity 0x03 and code 0x0001 is PTM_NONDETERMINISTIC.
2860 Category 0x06 with severity 0x03 and code 0x0002 is PTM_PROHIBITED_HEAD_MOVE.
2861 Category 0x07 with severity 0x02 and code 0x0001 is COMPLEXITY_UNPROVEN.
2862 Category 0x07 with severity 0x02 and code 0x0002 is COMPLEXITY_HARD_CODED.
2863 Category 0x08 with severity 0x03 and code 0x0001 is QUIPPER_NON_UNITARY.
2864 Category 0x08 with severity 0x03 and code 0x0002 is QUIPPER_OPENQASM_PRIMARY.
2865 Category 0x09 with severity 0x03 and code 0x0001 is JCL_PATH_ESCAPE.
2866 Category 0x09 with severity 0x03 and code 0x0002 is JCL_DUPLICATE_DATASET.
2867 Category 0x0A with severity 0x03 and code 0x0001 is AUDIT_HASH_MISMATCH.
2868 Category 0x0A with severity 0x03 and code 0x0002 is AUDIT_TIMESTAMP_FABRICATED.
2869 Category 0x0B with severity 0x03 and code 0x0001 is VALIDATION_BYTECODE_INVALID.
2870 Category 0x0B with severity 0x03 and code 0x0002 is VALIDATION_STACK_OVERFLOW.
2871 Category 0x0C with severity 0x03 and code 0x0001 is TEST_ASSERTION_FAILURE.
2872 Category 0x0C with severity 0x03 and code 0x0002 is TEST_TIMEOUT.
2873 Category 0x0D with severity 0x04 and code 0x0001 is SECURITY_SANDBOX_ESCAPE.
2874 Category 0x0D with severity 0x04 and code 0x0002 is SECURITY_CREDENTIAL_LEAK.
2875 Category 0x0E with severity 0x04 and code 0x0001 is SANDBOX_SYSCALL_VIOLATION.
2876 Category 0x0E with severity 0x04 and code 0x0002 is SANDBOX_FD_VIOLATION.
2877 Category 0x0F with severity 0x03 and code 0x0001 is RESOURCE_CPU_EXHAUSTED.
2878 Category 0x0F with severity 0x03 and code 0x0002 is RESOURCE_MEMORY_EXHAUSTED.
2879 Category 0x10 with severity 0x03 and code 0x0001 is IO_READ_ERROR.
2880 Category 0x10 with severity 0x03 and code 0x0002 is IO_WRITE_ERROR.
2881 Category 0x11 with severity 0x04 and code 0x0001 is NETWORK_SOCKET_CREATED.
2882 Category 0x11 with severity 0x04 and code 0x0002 is NETWORK_CONNECTION_ATTEMPTED.
2883 Category 0x12 with severity 0x03 and code 0x0001 is MEMORY_ALLOCATION_FAILED.
2884 Category 0x12 with severity 0x03 and code 0x0002 is MEMORY_ALIGNMENT_ERROR.
2885 Category 0x1B with severity 0x04 and code 0x0001 is DETERMINISM_HASH_MISMATCH.
2886 Category 0x1C with severity 0x04 and code 0x0001 is REPRODUCIBILITY_FAILURE.
2887 Category 0x1D with severity 0x03 and code 0x0001 is HASH_MISMATCH_ARTIFACT.
2888 Category 0x1D with severity 0x03 and code 0x0002 is HASH_MISMATCH_CONFIG.
2889 Category 0x1E with severity 0x03 and code 0x0001 is VERSION_INCOMPATIBLE.
2890 Category 0x1F with severity 0x03 and code 0x0001 is MAGIC_UNEXPECTED.
2891 Category 0x20 with severity 0x03 and code 0x0001 is CHECKSUM_HEADER_FAIL.
2892 Category 0x20 with severity 0x03 and code 0x0002 is CHECKSUM_PAYLOAD_FAIL.
2893 Category 0x21 with severity 0x03 and code 0x0001 is ENCODING_INVALID_BYTE.
2894 Category 0x21 with severity 0x03 and code 0x0002 is ENCODING_TRANSCODE_FAIL.
2895 Category 0x22 with severity 0x03 and code 0x0001 is ALIGNMENT_UNALIGNED_ACCESS.
2896 Category 0x23 with severity 0x03 and code 0x0001 is RESERVED_BITS_NONZERO.
2897 Category 0x24 with severity 0x02 and code 0x0001 is ORACLE_UNSTATED.
2898 Category 0x25 with severity 0x03 and code 0x0001 is UNITARY_TOLERANCE_EXCEEDED.
2899 Category 0x26 with severity 0x03 and code 0x0001 is CYCLE_DEPENDENCY.
2900 Category 0x26 with severity 0x03 and code 0x0002 is CYCLE_CALL_GRAPH.
2901 Category 0x27 with severity 0x04 and code 0x0001 is CREDENTIAL_IN_OUTPUT.
2902 Category 0x28 with severity 0x04 and code 0x0001 is SECRET_IN_OUTPUT.
2903 Category 0x29 with severity 0x04 and code 0x0001 is SANDBOX_ESCAPE_DETECTED.
2904 Category 0x2A with severity 0x04 and code 0x0001 is ARBITRARY_COMMAND_EXECUTED.
2905 Category 0x2B with severity 0x04 and code 0x0001 is CREDENTIAL_INFERENCE_ATTEMPTED.
2906 Category 0x2C with severity 0x03 and code 0x0001 is HARD_CODED_RESULT_DETECTED.
2907 Category 0x2D with severity 0x03 and code 0x0001 is OPENQASM_USED_AS_PRIMARY.
2908 Category 0x2E with severity 0x03 and code 0x0001 is LAYER_COLLAPSE_DETECTED.
2909 Category 0x2F with severity 0x03 and code 0x0001 is SEMANTIC_CHANGE_DETECTED.
2910 Category 0x30 with severity 0x03 and code 0x0001 is PROVENANCE_MISSING.
2911 Category 0x31 with severity 0x04 and code 0x0001 is TIMESTAMP_FABRICATED_DETECTED.
2912 Category 0x32 with severity 0x03 and code 0x0001 is UNKNOWN_ERROR.
2913 The error handling protocol file hash is SHA-256 over the complete file.
2914 The error handling protocol file hash is recorded in the audit manifest.
2915 The error handling protocol is validated before use.
2916 Error handling protocol validation checks magic, version, header checksum, and payload length.
2917 Error handling protocol validation checks that all category indices are unique.
2918 Error handling protocol validation checks that all handler indices are unique.
2919 Error handling protocol validation checks that all recovery indices are unique.
2920 Error handling protocol validation checks that handler categories are within defined range.
2921 Error handling protocol validation checks that recovery trigger categories are within defined range.
2922 Error handling protocol validation checks that error codes follow the structured layout.
2923 Error handling protocol validation checks that severity values are within [1, 4].
2924 Any error handling protocol validation failure triggers a fallback to default error handling.
2925 Default error handling is: log the error, abort the phase, record in audit trail.
2926 The error handling protocol does not execute arbitrary commands.
2927 The error handling protocol does not access network resources.
2928 The error handling protocol does not modify files outside the vault.
2929 The error handling protocol does not infer or emit credentials.
2930 The error handling protocol treats all error conditions as untrusted.
2931 The error handling protocol is deterministic.
2932 The same error condition always produces the same handler sequence.
2933 The error handling protocol is part of the vault read-only assets.
2934 The error handling protocol configuration hash is recorded in the audit manifest.
2935 The error handling protocol is fail-closed.
2936 The error handling protocol does not allow graceful degradation on security errors.
2937 Security errors (categories 0x0D, 0x0E, 0x11, 0x27, 0x28, 0x29, 0x2A, 0x2B, 0x31) always trigger containment.
2938 Containment on security errors is immediate and does not pass through recovery procedures.
2939 The error handling protocol is the final authority on pipeline failure modes.
2940 No pipeline component may override the error handling protocol.
2941 The error handling protocol is loaded before any user code executes.
2942 The error handling protocol is verified for integrity at load time.
2943 The error handling protocol integrity verification uses SHA-256.
2944 The error handling protocol integrity verification compares against the recorded hash in the audit manifest.
2945 Hash mismatch at load time triggers a fatal error and pipeline abort.
2946 The error handling protocol is the safety net for all pipeline operations.
2947 The error handling protocol is documented in this specification.
2948 The error handling protocol is tested by the TEST_MALFORMED_INPUT and TEST_SANDBOX_VIOLATION test cases.
2949 The error handling protocol is reviewed as part of the conformance report.
2950 The conformance report includes a section on error handling protocol compliance.

2978 All artifact types use big-endian byte ordering unless explicitly stated otherwise.

3001 The pipeline consists of 13 phases: lexical, syntactic, semantic, type system, control flow, PTM lowering, complexity analysis, Quipper generation, JCL validation, sandbox verification, execution, testing, audit.
3002 Phase 1 (lexical) reads ART_SOURCE_CODE and produces ART_TOKEN_STREAM.
3003 Phase 2 (syntactic) reads ART_TOKEN_STREAM and produces ART_PTM_IR.
3004 Phase 3 (semantic) reads ART_PTM_IR and validates symbol tables and expressions.
3005 Phase 4 (type system) reads ART_PTM_IR and validates type descriptors and consistency.
3006 Phase 5 (control flow) reads ART_PTM_IR and produces ART_CFG.
3007 Phase 6 (PTM lowering) reads ART_PTM_IR and ART_CFG and produces ART_PTM_TRANSITION.
3008 Phase 7 (complexity analysis) reads ART_PTM_TRANSITION and produces ART_COMPLEXITY_REPORT.
3009 Phase 8 (Quipper generation) reads ART_PTM_IR and ART_CFG and produces ART_QUIPPER.
3010 Phase 9 (JCL validation) reads ART_JCL_MANIFEST and validates dataset paths and policies.
3011 Phase 10 (sandbox verification) reads ART_SECURITY_BOUNDARY and verifies all checks.
3012 Phase 11 (execution) reads all artifacts and executes the pipeline steps.
3013 Phase 12 (testing) reads ART_TEST_VECTOR and executes all test cases.
3014 Phase 13 (audit) reads all artifacts and produces ART_AUDIT_TRAIL and ART_CONFORMANCE_REPORT.
3015 Each phase has a validation checkpoint before the next phase begins.
3016 Each checkpoint validates all rules in the validation protocol for that phase.
3017 Each checkpoint produces a validation event record in the audit trail.
3018 Each checkpoint must pass before the next phase is allowed to start.
3019 A checkpoint failure triggers the error handling protocol.
3020 The error handling protocol determines whether to retry, skip, or abort.
3021 The canonical example flows through all 13 phases successfully.
3022 The canonical example produces all artifact types.
3023 The canonical example passes all validation checkpoints.
3024 The canonical example passes all 50 test cases.
3025 The canonical example produces a conformance report with status CONFORMANCE_PASS.
3026 The canonical example demonstrates the complete pipeline from source to final report.
3027 The canonical example is used as the first end-to-end fixture.
3028 The canonical example is not hard-coded into the pipeline logic.

3030 The pipeline generalizes to arbitrary loop bounds, not just 1 to 100.
3031 The pipeline generalizes to arbitrary variable names, not just I and SUM.
3032 The pipeline generalizes to arbitrary arithmetic expressions.
3033 The pipeline generalizes to arbitrary control flow structures.

3036 The pipeline generalizes to arbitrary procedure calls.

3040 The pipeline maintains separation between classical and quantum semantics.
3041 The classical PTM execution is independent of the Quipper artifact.
3042 The Quipper artifact is optional and does not affect classical execution.

3047 The pipeline uses explicit complexity bounds for all transformations.
3048 The complexity bound T(n) is stated in the complexity report for each phase.
3049 The complexity bound S(n) is stated in the complexity report for each phase.
3050 The complexity bound uses the input encoding defined in Section 0000.
3051 The input encoding is binary encoding of the PTM-IR file.
3052 The input size n is the bit length of the PTM-IR file.
3053 The bit length is 8 times the byte length.
3054 The byte length is recorded in the PTM-IR file header payload length field.
3055 The complexity analysis phase computes n from the PTM-IR file header.
3056 The complexity analysis phase computes T(n) by analyzing the transition table.
3057 The complexity analysis phase computes S(n) by analyzing the tape usage.
3058 The complexity analysis phase records c, d, and n0 in the complexity report.
3059 The complexity analysis phase validates that T(n) <= c * n^d for all n >= n0.
3060 The complexity analysis phase does not claim polynomial time for non-polynomial algorithms.
3061 The complexity analysis phase distinguishes between input-dependent bounds and source constants.
3062 A source constant loop bound produces O(1) complexity with respect to input size.
3063 An input-dependent loop bound may produce polynomial or higher complexity.
3064 The complexity analysis phase is conservative: it does not over-claim polynomial bounds.
3065 If a polynomial bound cannot be proven, the complexity report states UNPROVEN.
3066 If a polynomial bound is disproven, the complexity report states DISPROVEN.
3067 The complexity report is validated against the transition table.
3068 The complexity report validation checks that d matches the transition table header field.
3069 The complexity report validation checks that c is a positive finite number.
3070 The complexity report validation checks that n0 is a positive integer.
3071 The complexity report validation checks that the input encoding is explicitly stated.
3072 The complexity report validation checks that the proof text references the specific algorithm.
3073 The complexity report validation is fail-closed.
3074 The pipeline uses deterministic serialization for all artifacts.
3075 Deterministic serialization means identical inputs produce identical byte sequences.
3076 Deterministic serialization requires stable field ordering.
3077 Deterministic serialization requires stable array ordering.
3078 Deterministic serialization requires zero padding.
3079 Deterministic serialization requires no timestamps in non-audit artifacts.
3080 Deterministic serialization requires no randomness in non-quantum artifacts.
3081 Deterministic serialization is validated by the TEST_DETERMINISM test case.
3082 The pipeline uses fail-closed validation for all artifacts.
3083 Fail-closed validation means any validation error stops the pipeline.
3084 Fail-closed validation does not allow partial artifact publication.
3085 Fail-closed validation does not allow skipped validation rules.
3086 Fail-closed validation does not allow disabled security policies.
3087 Fail-closed validation treats warnings as non-fatal but records them.
3088 Fail-closed validation treats errors as fatal and aborts the pipeline.
3089 Fail-closed validation treats fatal errors as immediate aborts.
